**26. No image library — read the header** — a thumbnail check is not worth a native dependency; both accepted formats put dimensions in the header. [thumbnail-validate.ts:19-25]

**27. Validators never fix, and re-run at use time** — "the fix is Owen re-exporting"; the path points at an external volume, so "it validated when I picked it" says nothing about now. [thumbnail-validate.ts:8-18; audio-validate.ts:9-24]

**28. Spreaker limits pinned from the help centre** (300MB — the API guide states none); OAuth2 only, `basic` scope only; nothing retries (429 may mean IP blacklisting). [spreaker-push.ts:26-50]

**29. Spreaker push is a CREATE, not an UPDATE** — pushing twice publishes a SECOND episode; `spreakerEpisodeId` present = refuse. [spreaker-push.ts:9-23]

**30. Video matcher: the draft filter became a LABEL** — a draft cannot be A/B tested, so the published case matters most; duration is a verification guard, not the key. [video-matcher.ts:10-21]

**31. Report resolution: recency beats picked titles** — a pick left on an older report used to drag the shelf backwards in time; reports linked to another video were dropped silently for years (the count now travels in the reason). [publish-bridge.ts:329-345]

**32. Carry-forward: what does NOT carry, with reasons** — refuses `publishAt` (about one upload), `videoId` (would aim the next push at a video this run never produced), `chosenTitles` (index a different run's list), `pushedAt`/receipt (would claim a push that never happened). "PARTIAL IS A STATED OUTCOME, NOT A FALLBACK." [carry-forward.ts:1-45]

**33. Selection migration never deletes** — unresolvable files MOVED to `selections/orphaned/` intact: "'we could not work out which item this belongs to' is a thing to say, not a thing to tidy away." [selection-migration.ts:1-31]

**34. `publishAt` must carry an explicit offset** — a bare local string is refused; the earlier behaviour silently stored the wrong hour across DST. [publish-types.ts:73-78]

**35. `filled` is deliberately NOT `published`** — filling puts text in the form; the operator presses Save. [publish-bridge.ts:487]

**185. Thumbnails pair themselves when the operator opens Metadata Reports, and that sweep NEVER replaces one (operator, 2026-09-07: "have the system automatically pair thumbnails with videos if i switch to metadata reports. i just ran some videos through but i hadn't made thumbnails yet because i needed the thumbnail text").** THE PROBLEM IS THE ORDER THE WORK HAPPENS IN, and it is structural rather than a bug: thumbnails are made AFTER generation because the operator needs the generated thumbnail text to make them, while the automatic pass that finds them (auto-config.ts) runs DURING generation, when the image does not exist. So a run finishes, the images land in `<week>/thumbnails/` an hour later beside `<week>/complete/`, and every item of that run sits with no thumbnail until "Look again" is clicked on each in turn. THE DECISION: the sweep runs on ARRIVAL at the reports page and again on the window REGAINING FOCUS while it is on screen — the second is the one that matters, because leaving for the image editor and coming back is exactly the shape of the work — and it **never replaces anything**. An item with any thumbnail path is not opened; nor is one whose source is `manual`, INCLUDING a manual clear, which the per-item rescan is deliberately allowed to undo and this is deliberately not: a click names an item, arriving at a page names nothing, and a sweep that could bring back an image the operator deleted by hand would make "remove this thumbnail" a state that ends whenever he navigates. Replacing stays the rescan's job, on the rescan's own stated reasons. ONE IMPLEMENTATION OF "FIND, VALIDATE, ATTACH", extracted rather than copied, and the rescan's own comment is the authority: these were two implementations once and "they drifted the moment one of them learned to shrink an oversized export — this button went on refusing files the automatic pass had started accepting, which from the outside was a button that did nothing." So `thumbnail-attach.ts` now holds the manual guard, `findUsableThumbnail`, the same-file check, the write and the sentence that reports it; the rescan calls it and keeps its exact behaviour, including its FIRST FILL (an item with no record gets one) which the sweep does not share. IT DOES NOT CREATE RECORDS: every item of every finished run is given one the moment the run lands (`attachPublishRecordsForJob`), so an item without one predates that pass, and routing a channel and deciding a destination for items the operator has never opened, on a call he did not make, is not what he asked for — those are counted and named in the log, and their first save runs the identical attach through the identical door. Items with no `sourcePath` — text subjects, compilations — are counted, not errors: the resolver says there is nowhere to look, in its own words. CHEAP BY DESIGN: one index listing, one already-cached record read per item, one `stat` per candidate path, and no image decoded for an item whose candidate file does not exist — which is every item that already has its image and every item that has none. It is fired and NOT awaited, so the list is on screen before it answers. DECLARED, NOT SILENT (law 8), and in both directions: a run that ATTACHED something says so on screen, naming the videos, because an image appearing on a row by itself with nothing to say why is exactly the silent automatic decision this app does not ship; a run that attached nothing says nothing, because it changed nothing. Every per-item reason — skipped, refused, no record, no image — is logged at info level in the main process, where it can be read when a row that should have paired did not; a hundred of them on screen would bury the one line that matters. Each attach records `thumbnailSource: 'auto'`, exactly as the automatic pass does, so the rescan and replace semantics downstream hold unchanged. CONCURRENCY: one sweep at a time, and a second request while one runs is DROPPED rather than queued — the second would find whatever the first has already attached and do nothing — and the drop is reported rather than hidden, because a page that asked and got nothing back deserves to know which of the two happened. It cannot interleave with a generation run: every write goes through `PublishStoreService.update`, which is one queue, and so does the automatic attach a finishing job performs; the job record is not touched at all, by either. Ordering is by construction rather than by a lock this call would have to remember to take. VERIFIED against the compiled dist through the REAL registered handler (setupPublishIpc, with `electron` faked so `ipcMain.handle` just records), on a scratch publish store and a fake week folder, in the operator's actual sequence: five items given records with NO thumbnails on disk; three 1280×720 PNGs then exported as `<slot> - youtube-thumbnail.png`; one of them claimed by hand. Sweep 1 attached 2, skipped 3 (the manual one, the item with no exported image, the item with no source path), refused 0, out of 5 considered. Sweep 2 attached 0. Two sweeps fired at once: the first ran, the second reported `dropped`. The manual item was untouched throughout. And the rescan still does what it did: pressed on an item whose automatic path is stale it REPLACED it, saying so ("replacing the automatically attached …"), and a sweep run afterwards attached nothing. NOT COVERED, deliberately: creating publish records (above); replacing anything (the rescan); a timer or a background poll — arrival and focus are the two moments the operator is looking at the rows, and a sweep nobody is watching announces its results to an empty page; and the calendar, which shows thumbnails too but is not where he comes back from making them. [thumbnail-attach.ts; publish-ipc.ts; preload.ts; electron.ts; publish.types.ts; metadata-reports.ts]

### Analytics / A-B

**36. "The A/B loop is an empty socket" was WRONG and corrected on the record** — PR #13 (2026-07-26) closed the loop end-to-end; 157 decided tests verified on disk. AB-TEST-PLAN.md's contrary text is stale and knowingly so. [TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md §3a]

**37. `liftPct` is POINTS, not a ratio** — `(+X% lift)` overstated ~3× at the stored values, in the one text whose job is teaching the model which titles win. Now `+8.6 pts watch-time share`, source lines pinned "so it does not get 'simplified' back." [TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md §3b]

**38. The 41% isWinner-vs-argmax disagreement was an ARTIFACT, twice corrected** — the store shows 0 of 157 disagreements; the exporter emits rows for UNDECIDED tests with winnerIndex defaulted to 0. Correct filter: `testOutcome == 'winner'`. Export holds 141 decided vs the store's 157, sets not nested — whether a backfill is needed at all is OPEN, pending a videoId set-difference nobody has run. [TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md §3b/3c]

**39. Three losing title traits, isolation-tested on 1,087 variants** — colon lost-alone 20/won-alone 5; question 15/2; digit 13/2. Naming a person is table stakes (90% of winners AND 91% of losers). Length decides nothing in the tested range. Reproduced across two export generations. [TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md §2]

**40. Measured effect of real head-to-heads in the prompt** — generic brief: 58% colon rate, 75% any losing trait; +521 curated titles: 17%/42%; +115 A/B head-to-heads: **0%/25%**. "Invisible by eye — the zero-shot titles look the most polished of any arm." [TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md §2]

**41. The length cap is untested by the A/B record, not refuted** — kept for mobile truncation; never sold as a performance rule. [§2]

**42. Two live prompts were instructing the losing shapes** — "Numbers and one [bracketed tag]…" and a QUOTA requiring one question-format title per batch (the shape that loses 15-to-2). "Fixing these three lines is probably worth more than the whole rules-derivation build." [§8]

**43. Fine-tuning tried and lost** — 9.4h QLoRA on qwen3.8:27b overfit inside the first 40% of one epoch (15/15 dialect cells agreeing); 9,721 rows but only 3,363 distinct titles ≈ 41k tokens against a 262k window — "there was nothing to compress." And training cannot curate: a prompt carries winners AND losers. [§7]

**44. Aggregate rules shipped as a TS port deliberately identical to the Python** — change one and the numbers stop reconciling; `\p{Nd}` not `\d` (Python's is Unicode-aware). Bands: STRONG ≥15 isolated losses AND ≥3:1; WEAK ≥8 AND ≥2:1; below → an observation, never a rule. [ab-title-rules.ts:11-95]

**45. The empty case is a NORMAL state, stated in words** — no single channel produces a strong rule (~272 tested videos per strong rule pooled); silence reads to a model as "nothing to avoid." Never substitute generic SEO advice. [§3d; ab-title-rules.ts:283-291]

**46. Never state a null result as an instruction — measured** — an arm told "length decided nothing" emitted a 101-character title, longer than any variant ever tested. Null findings render as observed ranges. [§4]

**47. Two traps that cost real time** — thinking models return empty content at tight budgets (`done_reason:"length"` with `content:''`); models preamble, so validate 15-110 chars and strip list numbering (a surviving "1. " also trips the digit check). [§4]

**48. The CSV format already drifted — read by column NAME** — 32 columns became 31 between generations; a positional reader would have kept producing plausible rules from the wrong column. [§4]

**49. CSV is NOT a transport into ContentStudio** — `POST /analytics/ab-tests` is live; the combined-CSV exporter serves a different consumer (the shipped web tool). Standing request: fail if a channel vanishes between exports. [§5]

**50. The exemplar block replaced an unbounded per-test dump** — ≤3 rules and 10 exemplars whatever the store size; insights written before the field existed THROW rather than shipping a prompt with no title evidence. [insights-prompt.ts:64-77]

**51. `removeAbTests` — an absence that was a defect** — the collector cannot retract; silence reads as "no news" to an upsert store, so a withdrawn learning kept feeding the generator. Callers must re-distil or the removal "would look done and change nothing the model sees." [analytics-store.service.ts:480-495]

**52. Ingest server: 127.0.0.1 only; CSRF = reject http(s) Origins; the bearer token is vestigial** (generated, displayed, enforced by no endpoint). Port collision records an error state, never picks another port. [ingest-server.service.ts:14-30]

**53. Analytics windows and floors** — 180-day insights window (stale feed impressions confound lifetime CTR); MIN_PACKAGING_IMPRESSIONS 1000 (below it CTR is meaningless and must never be an example to emulate); first-week snapshot 168h±48h; nulls excluded, never 0. [distillation.service.ts:12-40]

**54. `selectedArm` ≠ `winnerArm`** — conflating them invented winners for 22 of Telltale's tests (YouTube defaults undecided to arm 1). [AB-TEST-PLAN.md §8]

### Editor (port + services)

**55. Cardinal rules of the port: no fallbacks; port, don't rewrite** — ACS behavior is the spec wherever the doc is silent, comments and error messages included. [EDITOR-PORT-PLAN.md]

**56. The resolver chain that ended in a guess was DELETED** — ACS's "return the bare name and hope it's on PATH", the hardcoded conda path, the hardcoded /Applications bundle path: all gone. managed → repo layout → THROW naming the exact path. "An ffmpeg that is not the one this pipeline was built against fails later, somewhere unrelated, with a worse message." [binary-resolver.ts:10-22]

**57. `webSecurity: false` — editor window ONLY** — DOM media points at file:// which is cross-origin from both dev http and packaged file pages; the main window keeps the default. [editor-window.ts:22-29]

**58. Editor window deliberately NOT a child window** — a parented BrowserWindow on macOS cannot be dragged to a separate display (DisplayLink virtual monitors included). [editor-window.ts:12-19]

**59. Archive sync: two deliberate omissions** — NO `--delete` (the user deletes local copies once archived; a mirror would erase the only surviving copy) and NO `--checksum` (reading 140GB on both sides to prove what timestamps already say). [archive-sync.ts:11-22]

**60. rsync, not copy** — a day folder is ~140GB that never changes after ingest. [archive-sync.ts:4-9]

**61. The sealed chapter-splitter method's laws** (2026-08-02, kept in the editor's story-splitting) — never emit a timestamp; temp 0 + format:json (0.50 → 0.00 on consecutive runs otherwise); invented neutral-domain names in examples (real names LEAK deterministically at temp 0); RANK ratings, never threshold (AUC ~0.55 alone, ranking doubles F1); over-segment then consolidate (over-splits are one click, under-splits unfixable); prompt bodies verbatim from the tested harness — "tested artifacts, not suggestions. Do not 'tidy' their wording." [chapter-splitter.ts:1-46]

**62. HOST/CLIP tagging in splitter stage 4, driven by a named failure** — audition3 showed attribution INVERTED on "racist flight attendant" (the host calling the PASSENGER racist). [chapter-splitter.ts:56-64]

**63. Ollama-only is a deliberate first step** — models already pulled locally; 127.0.0.1 over `localhost` (Windows reliability), the variant retried once "as a courtesy, never as a silent mask." [ollama-service.ts:4-14]

**64. Asset hosting moved repos without changing identity** — same artifacts, same sha256s: "identity is the checksum, not the URL." [asset-catalog.ts:17-22]

**65. editor-backend slimmed 2026-08-17** — non-arm64 and the bundled CPython removed; recovery path documented. [EDITOR-PORT-PLAN.md]

**66. One resolver per subsystem, not one shared resolver** — "beats one shared resolver with divergent semantics." [EDITOR-PORT-PLAN.md]

**67. Channel-name collisions resolved by namespacing (`editor:*`), no reuse-with-different-shape**; `sendToRenderer` targeting `getAllWindows()[0]` flagged as must-fix before a second window. [EDITOR-PORT-PLAN.md]

**68. Alignment audio never returns a fabricated envelope** — non-zero exit rejects with stderr tail; silent/empty rejects by name. "Project doctrine." [alignment-audio-service.ts:7-13]

**69. `shared-paths.ts` portable by design** — Node built-ins only; per-machine, not synced; Windows uses Local not Roaming. [shared-paths.ts:1-21]

**160. The editor transcribes on whisper large-v3-turbo (operator, 2026-08-24).** The heavier-model swap the base catalog entry's comment always promised. `whisper-large-v3-turbo` is the required catalog entry (sha256/bytes computed from the actual 2026-08-24 download, 1,624,555,275 bytes); `whisper-base` demoted to optional and kept so older installs still resolve. `getWhisperModelPath` prefers BOTH turbo rungs (managed, then bundled) over every base rung — a machine with managed base installed and a bundled turbo must run turbo or the swap never lands — and the transitional chain below stays, logged with the sidecar's model provenance. Verified before the entry was written: the bundled Metal whisper-cli loads turbo and transcribed a 4-second test clip verbatim in ~1.2s; the model is installed in the managed dir AND the dev-checkout models dir on this machine, so neither dev runs nor the setup screen re-download. Cost accepted: turbo is ~5x slower than base and 11x its size; transcription quality is the priority now. [asset-catalog.ts; binary-resolver.ts]

**182. Editor unfrozen for one session (Owen, 2026-09-03): stories are a verb, not a mode.** The Story tool/mode is gone; the pointer tools are Select and Blade. A story is made from the timeline highlight — ⌘S saves it as a new story, right-click on a lane offers Save as story / Add to story ▸ (hover list). The Stories tab picks (click, ⌘/Shift-click) and **Join**s (was "Merge"). Every story mutation is on the ⌘Z stack; snapshots carry `stories` as an optional field under the same no-schema-bump rule as `sequence`. Same session: the zoom floor is half-fit and the tail may scroll to viewport center (one `maxScrollOffset()` for clamp + scrollbar); a selection-duration badge rides next to the timecode; the processing modal's "Transcribe when processing finishes" (default on) queues the existing transcribe step behind the run and survives the modal closing; the archive auto-catch-up defers any week with a processing run inside it (rsync exit 24 on 2026-09-03 was the workflow's transient `*_ALTERED*.fcpxml` vanishing mid-push) and re-arms when the run ends. [editor.component.ts; project-setup-modal; project-sidebar.component.ts] **Merge lag, recorded (2026-09-10):** this batch sat on `feat/editor-stories-and-timeline` unmerged and unpushed for a week while six unrelated commits landed on main, so the app Owen was running still had the Story mode and no transcribe option — he reported the work as "missing". The two sides touched disjoint files and the merge was clean; all three builds pass. The lesson is the branch, not the code: an unmerged branch is invisible to the person running the app, and "built and green" is not "shipped".
**186. The highlight became adjustable, and the playhead became grabbable (Owen, 2026-09-11: "highlight something, then grab the edges of that highlight and move the highlighted region out to highlight more"; "more forgiving so i can ... find where a story starts or ends, and then grab the highlighted edge").** Both ends of the timeline highlight are now handles (ruler tab + full height of the band, `selectionEdgeAtX` / `selEdgeDrag`), and the playhead can be grabbed anywhere in the track lanes, not only on the ruler strip — the scouting gesture that used to replace the highlight on the way. Dragging an end soft-snaps to cut boundaries AND to the playhead, which wins outright whenever it is in reach. Escape mid-drag restores the pre-grab highlight; a resize moves no footage so it is NOT an undo step. Operator chose "draggable playhead, clip click unchanged" over two alternatives that would have retrained the section-select click. THE THREE CORRECTIONS ALL CAME FROM DRIVING THE RUNNING APP over CDP (Input.dispatchMouseEvent + `ng.getComponent` state reads, against a symlinked copy of a real session so no real project could be written), not from reading the code: (1) at fit zoom on a 3½-hour session a section is ~3px wide and a flat ±7px reach let one hairline highlight swallow every press for 17px around it — each end's reach is now capped at half the band's drawn width, and a band under 2px has no handles and no drawn tabs; (2) a section click parks the playhead a few px inside the left edge and a handle pulled to the playhead lands on the same pixel, so nearest-wins arbitration with a 1px bias toward the handle; (3) cut boundaries are dense enough that one is usually a millisecond nearer than the playhead. Also: a drag may not pinch a band below the few px its own handles need, because a highlight you cannot grab back is a dead end. 16 checks green in the app. Untested by Owen. [editor.component.ts, timeline-renderer.ts]
**187. Compilation packaging was the last call the Settings "AI Model" field still governed, and a truncated Claude answer was being reported as no answer (Owen, 2026-09-13: "i'm getting a 'no response from AI' message when i have claude -p selected in routing").** Two bugs stacked behind one wrong message. (1) NOT ROUTED: `generateCompilationMetadata` called `runMetadataRequest` with no model, so the one whole-metadata call a compilation makes fell through to `metadataModel` — the legacy Settings picker the per-item legacy path was killed for. A routing table set entirely to `claude -p` still sent it to the metered API, and the run's only clue was a log line naming a model nobody had chosen. It now follows the `titles` selection (`resolveCompilationPackagingOption`), the way the summarizer follows `chapters`; `routedModelString` prefixes a local option for the transport, since `makeRequest` rejects the bare Ollama name the table stores. The ipc-handlers comment claiming Settings governs packaging went with it — that sentence is how a forgotten setting keeps governing things. (2) A CEILING SIZED FOR A MODEL THAT DOES NOT THINK: `max_tokens` was 16000 on the plain path and 8000 on the JSON path, on the assumption that a call which does not ASK for thinking does not get any. Measured false against the live API the same day: `claude-sonnet-5` with NO `thinking` parameter returns a `thinking` block and, at a ceiling it cannot finish inside, NO text block at all — `stop_reason: max_tokens`, the whole budget spent, `content.find(type==='text')` undefined. That null travelled up as "No response from AI", was retried identically, and killed the run twice (17:30, 17:31); the same call had squeaked in at output=7336 on 2026-09-06. One ceiling now, 16000, with `thinking: adaptive` named explicitly on both paths — it is the mode these models run anyway, and a request that receives adaptive thinking without naming it is a request nobody sized. (3) A TRUNCATED ANSWER NOW THROWS, naming the ceiling, the output tokens and whether any text survived, instead of warning and handing the caller a JSON fragment, a description cut off mid-sentence, or null. The empty-answer contract is unchanged for a call that RAN TO COMPLETION: `end_turn` with no text still returns null and stays this field's decision rather than the run's. The Ollama `done_reason=length` check still only warns — a separate transport, a separate decision. Verified: both check suites green, and the API probe that proved the mechanism is in the session transcript. UNTESTED END-TO-END — no compilation has been run through the fixed path. [ai-manager.service.ts, metadata-routing.ts, metadata-generator.service.ts, ipc-handlers.ts, tools/routing-publish-checks.js]

**188. Stream marks: the Notepad file becomes a feature, and the marks become stories (Owen, 2026-09-13: "i currently use notepad to mark down when my timestamps are ... click a start button when i start streaming, and then ... hit a button when i finish a story and ... go back and fill in what that story is named roughly").** While he streams he writes down the moment each story ENDS and a word or two naming it; the next day the stream master goes into the timeline editor and he redraws those boundaries by hand. Four pieces replace both halves of that. (1) A MAIN-PROCESS STORE, one JSON file per session under `<userData>/stream-marks/`, written through on every change and broadcast to every window as `stream-marks:changed` — no cache, no polling, because two windows and a hotkey are three writers and the file is the only one of them that survives a crash. (2) A GLOBAL HOTKEY, `CommandOrControl+Shift+M` by default and stored as `streamMarksHotkey`, so a mark costs nothing during a live stream: no window switch, no focus, a desktop notification ("Mark 4 at 01:12:33") as the only feedback. **THE FIRST PRESS IS THE START, AND ONLY THE START** — with nothing live the key starts a session and drops no mark (a mark at 00:00:00 would be a boundary nobody pressed: row 1 on the tab, an empty story in the import; the first build did exactly that and was corrected in review); every press after it is a mark at the elapsed time. `addMark()` with no live session refuses, naming Start. So there is no button to remember to press before the night begins. Registration status is a VALUE, not a log line (`stream-marks:hotkey-status`, green "ready" or the OS's refusal in red, with the accelerator editable on the page): `globalShortcut.register` returns false when another app owns the combination, and a silent false is a night of stories with no boundaries. (3) A "STREAM MARKS" TAB between Editor and Analytics: running clock, Mark/End, an editable wall-clock start, and the marks most-recent-first with an editable `hh:mm:ss`, an editable label, −1m/−30s/+30s nudges for the "I realised late" case, insert-before and delete. Write-through on every edit, no Save button — a Save button on a page a hotkey is also writing to is a button that eventually overwrites a mark. Nothing is coerced: a time that will not parse keeps the typed text, says so under the row and writes nothing, because a boundary read as a different number is a story that starts in the wrong place and nobody finds out until the video is cut. A hotkey mark focuses its label only when the window is looked at again; a button mark focuses it at once. (4) AN EDITOR DIALOG, "Stream marks…", beside the Stories tab's other controls. **THE DECISIONS THAT SHAPE IT.** *Marks are boundaries, not story ends*: story i runs from the previous mark (or 0) to mark i and takes mark i's label, and the material after the LAST mark is not a story because nothing ended it. *Wall clock is stored so the file system can propose the offset*: `session.startedAt − master birthtime` in seconds, editable as `±hh:mm:ss`, with "Selected mark = playhead" as the escape hatch — one correction slides every story together, because they all share the one offset. `birthtime` is reported as NULL when the filesystem keeps none (it answers with the epoch) and the dialog then SAYS it is dating the master by its modification time; it is display text, never a silent substitution. *Shifting `startedAt` does not touch the marks' elapsed `at` values*, and that is the feature: the error being corrected is "I hit Start five minutes late", and holding the elapsed times still is exactly what makes one edit move the whole night. Every row shows what it would create before anything exists — clamped rows flagged and trimmed, wholly-off rows disabled with the reason — and Apply mints them with ONE `pushUndo` for the batch, in stream order, through the same six steps as `saveSelectionAsStory` so the claim rule keeps stories non-overlapping however they were made. The arithmetic (marks→spans, offset, strict `hh:mm:ss`/`±hh:mm:ss`) is a pure module with a spec, shared by both screens — one parser, because a second one agrees until the day it learns to accept "1:2:3". The editor's port gained the group as OPTIONAL: a host with no marks store implements none of it and the button does not appear. VERIFIED against the compiled dist on a scratch store — first-press-is-start, elapsed times unmoved by a `startedAt` correction, re-sorting on edit, a corrupt file and two-live-sessions both throwing by path, list newest-first — and the pure module's assertions run green; the Karma suite itself could not be run (`electron.spec.ts` imports a class name that no longer exists, pre-existing). UNTESTED BY OWEN, and no real stream has been through it. **First real import, same week: the file time was the END.** Owen's first run opened with all six stories disabled and every row before 00:00:00 — a 3h16m master created at 23:13 for a stream that began at 19:57, i.e. the master's creation time was the moment the recording FINISHED (a rendered, downloaded, copied or moved file carries a time at or after the end; only a recorder writing into its own open file stamps the start, and macOS gives a copy the copy's creation time). The dialog now offers BOTH readings as a toggle — under "ended" the recording's start is the file time minus the timeline's length — preselects whichever lands more stories on the timeline, and SAYS which it picked and why ("reading it as the start leaves 6 of 6 stories off this timeline"). Verified against those exact numbers: start → offset −03:16:44, 0/6 creatable; end → offset +00:00:01, 6/6 inside. The bug was not the arithmetic but a single unstated assumption presented as a proposal. **Offset nudges, and the start-time rule reversed by the first real night (Owen, 2026-09-15: "its definitely offset by about 2 - 3 minutes. each story starts 3 minutes late ... i need some way to shift the offset").** Diagnosed on his actual files, not by reasoning: the session store said Start was pressed at 19:59:39 and `startedAt` was later corrected to 19:57:00, the master ffprobes at 11785.152s with mtime=birthtime=23:13:44 (one write, so the stamp is the END), and the fcpxml sequence is 11785.140s — timeline and master are the same length, so the anchor, not the arithmetic, was wrong. TWO CHANGES. (1) THE OFFSET IS A DIAL: −5m/−1m/−10s/+10s/+1m/+5m beside the typed field, because the proposal is an estimate with error at both ends — a file is stamped when its WRITE finished (18s after his stream ended) and the timeline is only as long as the compound the editor was handed. (2) `updateSession(startedAt)` NOW MOVES THE ELAPSED TIMES, reversing #188 — a mark is a key press at a wall-clock INSTANT and the elapsed number is a rendering of it, so correcting the start by −2m39s adds 2m39s to every mark and each one stays where it happened (verified: press at 21:05:06 still reads 21:05:06; gaps between marks, and so story lengths, unchanged; story 1 grows, which is right because the stream really was running before the first press). Holding them still — the rule #188 called the feature — is what silently moved his six stories ~3 minutes from their content. A correction that would push a mark before the new start is refused by name, never clamped. His existing session predates the fix and is repaired in the dialog: park the playhead where a story really ends, click that row, press "Selected mark = playhead". [stream-marks.service.ts, stream-marks-ipc.ts, main.ts, preload.ts, editor-ipc.ts (MASTER_PATTERN exported), electron.ts, stream-marks.types.ts, components/stream-marks/*, app.routes.ts, app.html, editor/model/stream-marks-import.ts + .spec.ts, editor/stream-marks-import-modal/*, editor-host.ts, editor-host.adapter.ts, editor.module.ts, editor.component.ts/.html/.scss]

**189. The stream-marks import was arithmetically incapable of being right, and the fix came from driving the real session (Owen, 2026-09-15: "shift buttons arent useful. we need to be able to grab the whole stream marks set and drag it left or right", then "its definitely offset by about 2 - 3 minutes").** THE MEASUREMENT FIRST, over CDP against a symlink sandbox of his 2026-09-14 project: the master ffprobes at 11785.152s, `manifest.timelineDuration` is 9869.16s, and the video track is **1954 segments** cut from that one master covering 9868.6s of it — the processing step drops 1916s (32 minutes) of dead air, so the editor's timeline is a NON-LINEAR remap of the recording, not a shifted copy. A single additive offset can be right at exactly ONE point on such a timeline. Mapping his six marks through the segment table against the six stories his linear import had produced: 20m36s, 17m13s, 12m30s, 5m09s, 2m35s, 25s of error, shrinking toward the end because he had tuned the offset until the LAST story landed. His "3 minutes late" was that curve sampled in the middle, and no amount of nudging could have removed it. THE FIX: a mark is a moment in the RECORDING, and placement is `masterToTimeline(segments, elapsed + streamStartInMaster)` — binary search over the table, its exact inverse `timelineToMaster` for the drag — with the one free constant being where the stream clock's zero sits in the master. Nothing is added to a timeline second anywhere. A mark that falls inside removed material is reported on its row and lands where the next kept piece starts, because a boundary placed in footage nobody will see is a boundary placed nowhere. The file-time proposal now uses the master's OWN ffprobed length (`durationSeconds` on `stream-marks:master-file-times`, spawned from runtime-paths' ffprobe, throwing with stderr) rather than the timeline's, since those differ by exactly the removed 32 minutes. THE GESTURE, which is what he asked for: the dialog collapses to a bar at the foot of the window (the editor root reserves its height — the zoom slider and scrollbar are wanted WHILE aligning), every boundary draws on the canvas as a cyan line with its story's band and title, and a press anywhere below the ruler grabs the boundary nearest the pointer. The grab is RELATIVE and tracks the cursor ONE FOR ONE in timeline pixels (two rejected alternatives, both corrected during verification: an absolute grab teleports the nearest line under the cursor on mousedown, and measuring the travel in the recording's seconds lets the line drift from the hand as the density of removed air changes); the other boundaries move to wherever their own content sits, which is the non-linearity being honest. The ruler still scrubs, because the playhead is the reference. Snap 7px to blades and the playhead on the grabbed boundary only, Option bypasses, Escape restores the constant from grab time, release commits, no undo step — Apply is still the one mutation and still a single `pushUndo`. The ±5m/±1m/±10s nudge buttons added that morning are DELETED. VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING APP against the sandbox: proposal −00:00:20 with 6/6 rows landing (identical to an independent recomputation over the 1954-segment table); a 120px drag at 0.3px/s moved the grabbed boundary exactly 400 timeline seconds while its neighbours moved 395/448/391/420 and the last clamped at the end; aiming 16.7s short of the playhead landed on it exactly; Escape restored the constant to the digit; Apply created 6 stories and one ⌘Z took them back. 32 pure-module specs green (Karma still blocked by the pre-existing `electron.spec.ts` import). Untested by Owen. [stream-marks-import.ts + .spec.ts, stream-marks-import-modal/*, editor.component.ts/.html/.scss, timeline-scene.ts, timeline-renderer.ts, stream-marks-ipc.ts, editor-host.ts, stream-marks.types.ts]

**190. Stories became the handle, and the line-up mode was deleted the day after it shipped (Owen, 2026-09-16: "if i hold shift and click+drag a story, all stories move. if i click+drag with no shift, it drags that one story left or right. if i click+drag a story edge, it just moves that story edge. if i shift+click+drag a story edge, it snaps to the edge of the story next to it, ignoring the cuts. then we can get rid of the stream positioning button completely").** #189's drag operated on a PREVIEW of stories that did not exist yet, in a mode with its own bar and its own overlay — a second way to do a job the ribbon could do on the real thing. Four gestures on the stories ribbon replace it: a block drag moves that story rigidly, shift+block moves EVERY story, an edge drag is unchanged (cut snapping + the elastic push), and shift+edge jumps that edge onto the facing edge of the neighbouring story, closing the gap exactly and ignoring cuts. Shift is read ONCE at grab time and the neighbour is resolved there too, so neither can change under the hand; a press under 3px is still a click (chunk select, deferred to mouseup so the highlight is not left on footage the story has left); one undo step per gesture, only if something moved. **THE FRAME EACH GESTURE MOVES IN IS THE LOAD-BEARING DECISION.** One story travels in TIMELINE seconds — it is a block you can see, and it keeps its on-screen width. The SET travels in RECORDING seconds (`masterToTimeline(timelineToMaster(edge) + deltaMaster)`, the delta taken at the grabbed story's anchor edge and clamped as a delta against the master's kept range, never per member): a uniform on-screen shift is exactly the arithmetic #189 proved cannot work on a timeline that drops 32 minutes of dead air, so the stories deliberately move by DIFFERENT on-screen distances. Measured in the running app on the sandbox: a 60.0s pointer travel moved one story +60.182s rigidly (width 1369.000 unchanged, neighbour yielded), and the same shift-drag moved the six stories 39.471/34.232/34.666/39.471/31.996/32.530s — an 11.9s spread, clamped as a set with the last story landing on the timeline's last frame. Edge drag: end 4594→4644.306 with the neighbour pushed to the identical value. Shift edge: a 200s gap closed to 0.000000, `story4.start === story3.end` bit-identical; with no neighbour on that side it tracks the pointer with no snapping, a deliberate absence stated at the call site. The map (`masterToTimeline`/`timelineToMaster`/`orderSegmentsBySource`, plus `masterKeptRange` for the set clamp) moved to `model/master-timeline-map.ts` with its specs — it is a property of the TIMELINE, not of stream marks. DELETED IN FULL: the "Line up on the timeline" button, the collapsed bar, the canvas preview overlay and its scene field, and every editor field, branch and cursor class that served them (a grep for all of them returns nothing). The import dialog keeps its picker, the started/ended toggle, the typed constant, "Selected mark = playhead" and Create. A shift-drag on a timeline the map cannot describe reports the reason and does not start — there is no fallback to a timeline delta. 35 pure specs green; three builds clean; all four gestures driven over CDP against a symlink sandbox, with the real project never opened. Untested by Owen. [editor.component.ts/.html/.scss, model/master-timeline-map.ts + .spec.ts, model/stream-marks-import.ts + .spec.ts, stream-marks-import-modal/*, timeline-scene.ts, timeline-renderer.ts]

**192. The calendar's runs were never stopped by leaving the page — only forgotten (Owen, 2026-09-18: "can we make the calendar page persistent so it continues uploading even if i flip away from the calendar page").** THE PREMISE WAS HALF WRONG AND THE BUG WAS WORSE THAN THE ONE REPORTED. Destroying a component does not cancel its async method, so the upload loop always ran to the end of its queue; `ngOnDestroy` cleared a timer and the progress listener and aborted nothing. What died was every signal DESCRIBING the run. Coming back mounted a fresh component with `uploadRun === null`, so the board read idle over a live run, the byte counter and the per-item results were gone for good, and — the part nobody had noticed — the Upload button re-enabled itself, because it is disabled on `uploadRun() !== null`. The main process refuses a duplicate of the item actually in flight (`activeUploads`) and of any item already carrying a videoId (youtube-upload.ts), so a second run could not have created a duplicate video, but it would have raced the first through the rest of the list and reported refusals for items that were fine. THE FIX IS WHERE THE RUN LIVES, not what it does: `PublishRunsService`, `providedIn: 'root'`, holding all three runs (YouTube, Spreaker, schedule push) and re-exposed on the component under the old names so the template is unchanged. Every rule came across verbatim — sequential, a failure does not stop the run, results published as the run goes, the two re-checks that make a Spreaker push irreversible. THREE THINGS CHANGED SHAPE. (1) The progress tick binds ONCE for the service's life instead of in `ngOnInit`: a listener torn down on navigation is exactly the thing being fixed. (2) The YouTube run's per-item re-check re-reads the index ITSELF rather than consulting the calendar's `scheduledChips`, because by the last item that component may have been destroyed two pages ago and never refreshed since — a stale computed is a worse answer than none. Where the old code's `reload()` failure emptied `entries` and silently refused every remaining item, a failed read now skips the check and lets the main process speak; it refuses an already-linked item by name anyway, so the honest degradation is one refusal per item rather than a whole run of them. (3) Completion is ANNOUNCED (`finished` = seq + `sweep`) instead of calling `reload()`/`refreshSweep()` on the component that started it — the starter may be gone, and whichever view is on screen answers for itself. `sweep` is carried rather than assumed because a Spreaker run must NOT sweep YouTube: three channels queried to confirm nothing happened. WHAT IT STILL DOES NOT SURVIVE: a window reload or an app restart. The loop is in the renderer, so a reload finishes the transfer already in the main process's hands and never sends the rest. Moving the queue main-side is the cure and is a different piece of work — the per-item re-checks are board rules and would have to go with it. No flag pretends to have noticed; the limit is stated in the service's header. Both builds clean, routing checks green. UNTESTED BY OWEN. **Brought to main 2026-09-27**, after #231: its "ready videos not in this run" warning needs a fresh read of the board, which the service does not have, so a FINISHED run leaves its item ids on the service and whichever calendar next reloads (the one on screen, or one opened later) takes them once and names the leftovers. [features/publish/publish-runs.service.ts (new), components/publish-calendar/publish-calendar.ts]

### Extension

**70. Recon was done on the wrong page — three live bugs, one root cause** — the standalone edit page differs from the upload wizard (different A/B dialog host, nav chips polluting `ytcp-chip`, paid promotion as a checkbox). "Any future recon must be done in the wizard." [AB-TEST-PLAN.md §3]

**71. `ytcp-chip` MUST be scoped to `#tags-container`** — 21 visible chips = 5 nav + 16 tags; an unscoped selector grabs "Videos", which has no delete icon — "exactly how the first live run failed." [AB-TEST-PLAN.md §3]

**72. The Studio DOM exposes the original filename the API withholds** — "the single most valuable finding": matching is an exact filename join, not fuzzy title similarity. [page.ts:1-7]

**73. ALL localhost traffic goes through the service worker** — a content-script fetch carries the page's Origin, which the CSRF whitelist rejects; "that rejection is correct and must not be relaxed." [publish-messages.ts:1-14]

**74. Content script stays in the ISOLATED world** — DOM nodes are shared; the one thing needing page globals is asked of the worker. [publish-content.ts:11-16]

**75. Two distinct write mechanics** — contenteditable needs `execCommand('insertText')`; the tags `<input>` needs the native value setter + Enter. Verified live: one write took the chip count 16 → 18. Tags REPLACE requires clearing the 16 channel-default chips first. [dom.ts:1-18]

**76. Selector policy: stable attributes over visible text** — SCREAMING_SNAKE radio names, `#delete-icon` not localized aria-labels; A/B variant slots located BY POSITION (aria-label is localized English, confirmation only). [fillers.ts:12-18]

**77. The nav strip re-pointed to Studio's own list** — tying it to the app made it go dark when the app closed and let collector coverage decide which videos the operator could reach. [nav-source.ts:1-17]

**78. The nav message is NOT a PublishMessage** — it never leaves the browser; it carries no videoId "an id in the message would be an invitation to trust it over the tab." [nav-messages.ts:1-20]

**79. The nav strip is NOT mounted in the upload wizard** — a hard navigation would drop half-entered unsaved state. [page.ts:30-40]

**80. Monetization is the extension's job because the API cannot do it** — no `monetizationDetails` write exists for a single-channel creator. NOT verified against live Studio (stated in-file); every miss returns a reason that dumps what it actually found; a half-understood radio group REFUSES. (2026-08-23: monetization is now always-on; the three-valued store type became literal `true`.) [monetization.ts:1-38]

**81. Fill actions are a registry** — monetization (0.2.1) was one entry plus its `surface` field; title/A-B fills LAST because its modal covers the rest of the form; every filler verifies its own write. [fillers.ts:1-18]

**82. The shelf is persistent, not a per-video prompt** — always mounted, never auto-fills; "that human review step is what keeps this 'assisted data entry by the account owner' rather than automation." [shelf.ts:1-18]

**83. Shelf and nav-strip prefs get separate storage keys** — collapsed for different reasons; `chrome.storage.local` not session ("a placement choice that resets on every restart is not a preference"). [nav-strip-prefs.ts, shelf-prefs.ts]

**84. Collector: record-and-continue isolation, no fallbacks** — channel-list failure stops the cycle (never collects against a stale list); per-channel failures recorded and skipped; columns matched by `.metric.type` never index; the injected function closes over nothing so it can be pasted into a live console. [background.ts:15-18; collector.ts:1-35]

**85. Outbox: no retries-with-degradation** — a failed push stays queued as-is; removed only after a successful POST. [outbox.ts:1-6]

**86. Brand/non-primary channels 403 without the delegation ALSO in headers** (`X-YouTube-Delegation-Context`, `X-Goog-AuthUser`, clientName 62) — corrected by live end-to-end test. [STUDIO-COLLECTOR-SPEC.md]

**87. Settings deliberately minimal** — port only; the channel list is pulled live from the app, shown read-only. [settings.ts:1-12]

**88. `resultState` wire format** — `CREATOR_EXPERIMENT_RESULT_STATE_*`; batches of 50 ids (100 → HTTP 400). [catalogue.ts:11-22]

**89. The A/B exporter's operational lessons** — skip-back-catalogue off by default (1,383 of 1,561 videos would lack titles); tab bounced through a blank page every 20 videos to reclaim renderer memory; channels scanned sequentially (request rate triggers throttling); Shorts labelled by `contentType`, never duration (one channel has 33 long-form videos under three minutes). [ab-test-exporter/README.md]

### Build / infra

**90. `dist-build` is a real directory again** — the symlink workaround existed for exFAT xattr corruption (AppleDouble files breaking ASAR integrity and signing); the volume is APFS as of Aug 2026. [prepare-build-output.js:1-13]

**91. Two Electron shims, on purpose** — the real-userData shim answers with real paths (for scripts that run the real pipeline); the /tmp stub is for pure-function checks. It hard-fails on non-darwin. [scripts/_electron-shim-real-userdata.js]

**92. `generate-metadata-cli` drives the app's own entry points** — nothing reimplemented; three loud overrides incl. `--assets` (the app reads a startup-installed COPY of the prompt tree — repo edits are invisible until restart; the CLI prints the divergence) and a transcript cache (Whisper deletes its SRT the moment it parses it). [scripts/generate-metadata-cli.js:1-25]

**95. Per-field generation measured live 2026-08-22** — six calls, two model loads; num_ctx pinned per model per run. Prompt sizes on record: legacy single call 22,063 chars → three-call 36,378 → per-field 73,516. [prompt-artifacts/PERFIELD-timings.md]

**96. `prompt-harness/variants/` deleted** — a variant is now a copy of the prompt tree. [prompt-harness/README.md]

**158. `--claude-cli`: the test CLI's fourth loud override (operator, 2026-08-24: "until we get this right, set it to claude -p so I'm not burning through API use on tests; just sonnet for now").** Every Claude call in a CLI test run goes through `claude -p --model sonnet` — the Claude Code subscription, never the metered key — via ONE patch point (everything cloud funnels through `AIManagerService.makeClaudeRequest`; the chapter service's `cloudPlain` IS `runPlainRequest`). Always sonnet whatever the routing named; `initializeClaude`'s billed connection test is skipped; a failed spawn throws with stderr. A transport swap for tests, not a fallback. Verified end-to-end on f3 thumbnail-text. [scripts/generate-metadata-cli.js]

**159. Chapter consensus sampling + whole-video name scaffold; title claim-attribution clauses (2026-08-30 overnight campaign, integrated same day on branch campaign-integration).** The campaign (ledgers and run-books in `/Volumes/Callisto/ContentStudio/.contentstudio/chapter-campaign/`) measured stage-1 boundary variance as the dominant chapter defect: the same prompt at provider defaults returned 19 vs 14 chapters on the same video, and slivers/missed plugs traced to it, not to wording. Shipped mechanism: on the LOCAL transport each stage-1 window is asked 5x at temperature 0.7 with the thinking pass off (/api/chat, think:false — with thinking on, one temp-0.7 sample reasoned for 15 minutes, verified on the first integration run), each sample's quotes are mapped exactly as before, and a boundary publishes on >=3-of-5 agreement (cluster 25s, median member). Cloud stays single-call (measured good 2026-08-24, and SAMPLES=1 is the same code path). The 2026-08-24 no-sampling-parameters and no-re-asks rulings stand for every other call; consensus is a deliberate repeat-ask design, not a retry, and the carve-out is documented at the constants. Also new: one whole-video name-scaffold call (standard spellings shown to every detail call — cross-chapter consistency is structurally impossible per-chunk), recent-titles threading into detail calls ("name what is NEW" — kills adjacent duplicate titles), and the titles.yml claims-as-claims + one-chapter-per-title clauses (order after craft lines is load-bearing: t006 measured craft language overriding safety language). Repo rollback point: tag `campaign-baseline-2026-08-30`. [chapter-whole-transcript.service.ts, plain-call.ts, chapter-transcript.ts voteBoundaries, pipeline/chapters.yml, fields/titles.yml, PROMPT-LEARNINGS.md Part 9]

**160. Same-day follow-ups to #159, from the first live runs.** (1) THINK POLICY: every local metadata call now runs thinking-off over /api/chat EXCEPT descriptions — the 27B's thinking pass burned entire output budgets four times in one day (stage-1 samples, insights distiller, titles, description), but descriptions measured WORSE thinking-off (the hook/blank-line/body shape collapsed), so that field keeps thinking and its NUM_PREDICT doubled to 8192 for reasoning headroom, the chapter stage's own pattern. (2) PROMO MATCH: the campaign register titles plugs "Promotion of the book ..." — the one word PROMO_PATTERN deliberately ignores (someone else's promotion is content) — so a plug stayed in the published list and drew a content sliver warning. promoMatch now also excludes a title that both speaks of promoting AND names one of the channel's own promoted_items (normalized, apostrophe-safe); threaded promoted_items into excludePromoChapters. Kent-Christmas-promotes-his-own-book style content titles stay content, verified against decoys. (3) Consensus opening-echo: boundaries within one cluster width of a window's start fold into it (a 5-second opening chapter shipped otherwise). [promo-chapters.ts, metadata-tasks.ts, description-unit.ts, chapter-whole-transcript.service.ts, plain-call.ts]

**161. Description thinking policy settled by measurement, and the spill warning made memory-aware (operator decision, 2026-08-30 afternoon).** The thinking-off experiment on descriptions is CLOSED, exclusion-tested: six contract wordings on the real assembled prompt (prose law, terse tail block, conflict removal, short context, two-line shape) never exceeded 2/6 format compliance — long-prompt structural compliance is inherent to the 27B thinking-off, not a wording problem (trivial prompts bind 4/4). Thinking-on is 100% in shape at ~4-5 min/call. Operator chose: keep thinking ON for descriptions AND chapter details (the two two-part output shapes, both tested with thinking through the pipeline's history); their reliability comes from budget + timeout (NUM_PREDICT 8192 reasoning headroom, CALL_TIMEOUT 600s — 300s was firing just before the 8192 budget could complete). Thinking-off stays for the line-shaped calls the campaign measured: consensus samples, name scaffold, titles, insights distiller. Also: numCtxGpuCeiling is memory-aware — the flat 12288 warned "slower" on every 16k run while ollama ps showed the model 100% on-GPU on the 64GB Studio; with 48GB+ unified memory the ceiling lifts to CTX_MAX and the false warning stops. [description-unit.ts, chapter-whole-transcript.service.ts, plain-call.ts]

**162. Descriptions ship thinking-off with a reshaped contract (operator: "turn thinking off, pack and ship", 2026-08-30 evening; supersedes the thinking-on half of #161).** The speed campaign measured the mechanism first: MTP builds are a dead lever on Metal (19.4 vs 20.3 tok/s), prefill is KV-cached across the three candidate calls (~free), and decode of 4.7-5k THINKING tokens at ~20 tok/s was ~95% of the 4-5 min wall — with a minimal 620-char rulebook the same model thinks 1.6-2.8k tokens, so the deliberation tracks the rule count superlinearly (description-ledger.md carries the arms). Rather than diet the Owen-curated rulebook under deadline, the shipped fix removes what thinking was buying: the layout. The contract is now ONE paragraph whose FIRST SENTENCE is the opening line (the rules always defined sentence 1 as the snippet), parsed by parseLeadBody (first sentence boundary after 30 chars, within the hook budget; no boundary throws loudly). parseHookBody and its blank-line layout are DELETED — thinking-off never produced the blank line (0-2/6 across six wordings) but produced the single-paragraph form in every measured run. Verified 4/4 offline at 18-62s/call (vs 240-450s thinking-on) plus an end-to-end CLI run; NUM_PREDICT back to an answer-sized 4096. The description rulebook-diet campaign is PAUSED with findings recorded — a future session can spend them. [description-unit.ts, plain-call.ts, pipeline/description.yml, description-ledger.md]

**163. Overnight UI-truth campaign (operator: "figure out where the errors/problems/false messages are and fix it all", 2026-08-31 night; inputs page explicitly untouched).** Three legs, all on main in revertible slices. (1) TAGS: the near-duplicate tags Owen hit were ASKED FOR — fields/tags.yml requested "the subject's name spelled the way people get it wrong", and the model misspelled whole organization names. Spreaker's variant now forbids misspellings and states the 20-cap; YouTube's allows exactly one, of the main subject's personal name. (2) LEGIBILITY: light-theme --text-muted was #adb5bd on white (2.3:1) under 9-10px type with 0.35-0.4 dimming — the "grayed out things I can barely read" had a measurable cause. Muted ink ~4:1, an 11px/0.7rem type floor on both big pages, disabled ink 0.38→0.52, dims 0.55-0.68. Error toasts persist until dismissed (were 5s, same as success). (3) MESSAGE TRUTH: three adversarial sweeps (calendar, reports, electron strings) + a regression review, every finding re-verified before fixing. Standouts: .mp4 sources were silently auto-routed isPodcast because routing read Spreaker's ACCEPTANCE list (video containers included) — routing now reads an audio-only list; a date moved on YouTube silently lost to the mirror (isOpenWork now surrenders only on same-instant, the Spreaker lane's rule, so divergence draws both rows); the Spreaker run counted lapsed dates it would refuse; confirm-panel excluded-groups tracked by duplicate keys (render crash in the panel that matters most); the push dialog promised "stays private until then" for a passed schedule; suggested-but-unrecorded channels showed a green tick over a button refusing "not routed". The recurring failure class, named: a message whose gating condition can hold while its claim is false, and a limit enforced only after the cost is paid. Both are now things the sweep checks for. **164. Visual pass with eyes on the app (operator, 2026-09-02: "the ui looks the same", "it's a complete mess, and I think font family is contributing", "lots of unnecessary screens — publishing is done from the calendar").** The night's campaign (#163) fixed truth and contrast, not looks; this pass was grounded in screenshots of the RUNNING Electron app, captured over the DevTools protocol (launch with `--remote-debugging-port=9222 --disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`; the second flag matters — Chromium stops painting a covered window and Page.captureScreenshot hangs; a plain browser at :4200 boots but has no data, since everything rides window.launchpad). Findings and fixes, three revertible commits: (1) FONTS — the theme names Inter but index.html loaded Roboto, and the Material prebuilt theme sets Roboto on 44 component tokens, so every button/select/menu/tooltip rendered in a second family; Inter is now what the page loads and every `--mat-*-font` token is repointed at `--font-family`; ten ad-hoc monospace stacks collapsed to one `--font-mono` token. (2) CALENDAR — the per-cell time label (four per row, under a column header already saying it) is gone; empty cells sit flat; the "Before today" strip listed uploaded past videos (eight, in warning colour, hiding the one lapsed row it exists for) and now lists only open work. (3) REPORTS — the Publish record tab is folded into a single view (operator chose "fold into one view" over keeping the tab or moving setup to the calendar): a compact Publish setup block keeps the ONLY controls for destination, monetization, thumbnail and episode audio; schedule is read-only with a jump to the calendar; the dispatch manifest, the inline month calendar, the Upload/Push/Send buttons and their receipts are removed from this page (publish-state and the dialogs untouched — the calendar uses them). Mono reserved for file names/paths/ids; tracked uppercase survives on three rules. ts 4304→3589, html 1751→1385. Open: the readiness strip (TITLES/CHANNEL/MONEY/WHEN/THUMB/LINK) and the setup block now state the same four facts twice, one to read and one to act on; merging them is the next cut.
