### Crucible migration (decided 2026-09-23; built per CRUCIBLE-MIGRATION-PLAN.md)

**193. All AI moves onto Crucible; claude -p stays outside (Owen, 2026-09-23).** Every model call (the Anthropic SDK, Ollama, `askOllamaPlain`, the editor's ollama-service, whisper.cpp in the pipeline and the editor, and voice isolation) goes through Crucible, one server per machine at :7100, the same as BookForge, Foundry and Briefcase. `claude-cli:` stays in `makeRequest`, the routing table and the test CLI as a transport outside Crucible ("it's a test value and isnt consistently used"). The routing table stays the source of model choice, each option mapped to a Crucible id. Acts: `generate` for every generation call, including scrub and Soften. Owen ruled ONE class, not write/rewrite: "if the only difference is the context limit then make it one class and give it the ability to set the context limit". `decide` is for snap. `analysis` goes to any server older than 1.0.24. [CRUCIBLE-MIGRATION-PLAN.md §0, §6]

**194. Keys live in Crucible, not the app (Owen, 2026-09-23).** `api-keys.json` is migrated into the local server's settings, then deleted. Keys are never pushed to a remote server without an explicit action. `openai:` is removed. No sampling parameter is ever sent to a cloud upstream. [plan §6.6]

**195. Two servers, park on busy, fast is a pin (Owen, 2026-09-23).** The Mac is the default. owens-pc (3090 Ti, vLLM) is a second, much faster Crucible server that work can be sent to when it's wanted done quickly. A busy or leased card parks the item with the holder's sentence. It never interrupts Owen's own PC work, never loops, and never falls back to CPU. One GPU lane per server, a lease per job per model, and an in-flight ledger with a startup and quit sweep. [plan §4, §13]

**196. Low context by default (Owen, 2026-09-23: "we should try to keep context limits low if possible. we CAN fit a whole video in, but it's significantly slower"; gated).** Every local call targets ≤12–16k tokens, and the load context is requested per run (Crucible 1.0.24: default 8k, a client-stated context up to a per-host ceiling, refused by name above it). After chapters, the field calls read the chapter digest instead of the raw transcript, **once the full-vs-digest A/B on Owen's videos passes**. This supersedes the "pass the whole thing in" half of the 2026-08-23 rule; Owen: "what we've established as the definitive 'best way' can replace the old way". A call over the loaded context refuses before sending and is never truncated. [plan §7]

**197. CPU-sized models stay local (Owen, 2026-09-23: "if its small enough to run on a CPU, it can stay local").** TitaNet speaker tagging is unchanged (in-process sherpa-onnx). nomic-embed-text moves off Ollama to run in-process (ONNX) with a whole-video centroid, replacing today's undeclared 20,000-character document cut (key-phrases.ts:73). The frequency fallback is deleted (Law 1; supersedes #107). [plan §12]

**198. ASR on Crucible, large-v3-turbo wherever possible (Owen, 2026-09-23).** → model choice superseded by **#203** (Qwen3-ASR); the rest stands. This covers the pipeline and the editor. The editor's word timings are adopted only after a comparison against today's whisper.cpp `-ml 1` on Owen's material. The filename-title seed carries over through Crucible's new `initial_prompt`. The PC runs large-v3 until Crucible has a cuda-linux turbo manifest. [plan §8]

**199. Chaptering at a chosen granularity on snap (Owen, 2026-09-23).** OUTLINE + ASSIGN + Viterbi replaces stage 1 of the fifth architecture once it beats it on Owen's published chapter lists. The three steps:
- **the 9B writes the outline.** Owen: "9b -> outline, outline -> snap, final chapter -> 27b -> chapter title";
- **snap (9B) assigns every sentence to an outline item.** The options are the outline items plus a fixed ad/self-promotion item, confirmed by a yes/no;
- **Viterbi** picks the path, with switch cost 20. Measured on YTSeg: F1@±1 0.72, Pk 0.21–0.23, count chosen automatically. The pairwise "new subject here?" question scored 0.59–0.61; the uniform baseline 0.25 / 0.44.

Each chapter's final title and summary come from `summarize_chapter` on the capable (27B) routed model. One service serves metadata chapters (detailed), editor Stories (broad: "it's just chapters for a livestream. broad chapters") and episode splitting (the coarsest; the splitter is broken and gets rebuilt on this). The Stories analyzer (chapter-splitter.ts, its inline prompts and ollama-service.ts), the episode splitter's dead path, and their prompts are deleted. Amends Law 6. Partly supersedes the "no 9B for chapters" routing rule. [plan §10]

**200. Voice isolation is a Crucible denoise job (Owen, 2026-09-23).** The vocals Mel-Band RoFormer becomes a second manifest under `denoise` (installed via `rvc`). It runs at 44.1 kHz, one chunk per job. Chunking and silent-chunk skipping stay client-side. voice-separator-env is removed after acceptance. [plan §9]

**201. Re-roll gate (Owen, 2026-09-23; later phase).** Snap yes/no rule checks (refers to Owen by name or as "the creator"/"the speaker", narrates the commentary rather than the subject, doesn't make sense). A failure is re-rolled at most 3 times with the failing reason passed back; the best attempt is kept, and after that it is delivered flagged, never blocked. It amends Law 3 as its one declared exception (approved 2026-09-23: "youre okay to amend the laws"). Title ranking is by relative comparison, validated against the 177 decided A/B tests before use. [plan §11]

**202. The editor is unfrozen for the Crucible migration (Owen, 2026-09-23: "yes, we can work on the editor").** G4 is lifted for this work: editor transcription, voice isolation, and the Stories analyzer becoming broad chapters. ⚠️ The inputs page (off-limits in the 2026-08-31 UI campaign) is NOT covered by this ruling; the queue changes that touch it (plan §14) wait on Owen. [G4; plan §8.2, §9, §10, §14]

**203. ASR is Qwen3-ASR-1.7B + Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B, fully (Owen, 2026-09-23: "we're switching over to qwen fully").** Supersedes #198's large-v3-turbo choice, for the pipeline and the editor. Why: Owen needs ums and uhs in the transcript with word times, so they can be cut later. Whisper drops them: the turbo transcript of the 2026-09-23 stream has 76 ums/uhs in 3.9 h. Qwen, with a verbatim-disfluency context prompt, gave about 70 ums and 34 uhs. Ten ums cut at the aligner's exact spans from the master were judged "flawless" by Owen. Measured on the Mac (bf16, MPS, qwen-asr 0.0.6): about 12x realtime alone. Known traps, relayed to Crucible: the default inference batch of 32 aborts MPS, so use 4; the footprint reaches about 41 GB; one repetition loop in 78 chunks collapsed 2,388 words onto one timestamp. The asr job must detect a loop and re-decode it, or fail loudly, never accept it silently (Law 1).

**204. The routing table is the only thing that picks a model (Owen, 2026-09-24: "it should not be using anything but claude -p for any ai calls ever if all routing is set to claude -p").** With every row on claude -p, a run failed with "credit balance is too low": AIManager's startup probe sent a paid Anthropic test call on the legacy Settings `metadataModel` before every run. The fixes:
- **No provider probe per run.** Clients are prepared in `makeRequest` on the model the call names.
- **`runMetadataRequest` requires a model.** It has no Settings default.
- **The episode splitter runs on the chapters selection.**
- **Tags is a visible dialog row.** It was hidden and pinned to the 9B.

Still off the table, pending Owen:
- **The editor Stories analyzer** keeps its own picker until P8 replaces it.
- **Key-phrase ranking** keeps calling `nomic-embed-text` through Ollama until P6 moves it in-process.

**205. The migration build starts (Owen, 2026-09-25).** Rulings taken at the start, on branch `crucible` off main (with #204's fix cherry-picked):
- **The inputs page UI is unfrozen** for this work. §14's queue rows (Fast toggle, the grey parked line, the lanes strip) go where the plan puts them.
- **The Mac's Crucible is open to agents** for acceptance runs; nothing on the Mac GPU needs asking. **The PC still needs Owen's go, every time** (Law 7 stands for the PC).
- **The editor Stories analyzer follows the chapters routing now**, before P8 replaces it. Its own Ollama picker goes; `story:suggest-title` follows the same selection.
- **`nomic-embed-text` is removed, not moved.** Owen: "we're getting rid of the nomic embed model and using the new chapters logic". P6 is deleted; key-phrase ranking goes with it. Tag pools come from the chapter list; an item with no chapter list gets its tags written by the tags routing.
- **No automatic hand-off between servers** (§21 Q14, Owen's Briefcase ruling applied): a busy server makes work wait with the reason. The **fast pin** is the only way work reaches the PC; `newJobsWaitFor: 'any'` is not built.
- **The official `qwen3-asr-1.7b` id, never `-mlx`** (§21 Q15): ContentStudio keeps ums and uhs to cut on, and the port drops them.
- Subagents build one phase each in their own worktree; every phase merges into `crucible` after review, and `crucible` merges to main when Owen accepts.

**206. No local whisper: every transcription is Crucible's `asr` job on `qwen3-asr-1.7b`, seeded with the item's metadata (Owen, 2026-09-25).** "no more local whisper. we're going with crucible for transcription too, and we'll be using qwen asr ... probably the big model, 1.7b, since we want accuracy." That covers the pipeline and the editor. The official id, never `-mlx` (#205). Every request states `language`, `word_timestamps:true`, `vad_filter:false` and a `context` (≤1,024 tokens) that carries the verbatim-disfluency instruction (#203) AND whatever the item already knows that could spell a proper noun right: the filename title, the job name, the channel's brand terms and promoted items from the prompt set, known speaker or guest names, and any existing description or title the item carries. Owen: "we should make sure to pass in any useful metadata with the prompt, too, in case it might have proper nouns spelled out correctly and it can act as a seed for proper nouns." whisper.cpp and its models leave the app in P10; until then nothing may call them.

**207. Wave 2 shipped on branch `crucible` (2026-09-25).** Six phases built by parallel agents, each merged after review, every check green (`check:pure`, `check:crucible` 19 keepers, `check:asr`, `check:chaptering` 42). The build notes are the record: `docs/crucible/{P1,P2,P3,P5,P7,P8a,stories-and-tags}.md`.
- **P1.** The SDK is vendored at 1.0.34 (`tools/adopt-crucible-release.mjs`, always naming the version). The registry is `<userData>/crucible-servers.json` plus `crucible-routing.json` `{selected, fastServer, paused}`: the user picks the primary server in Settings and every job goes there (Owen, 2026-09-25: "the user will pick which crucible server to use in settings. when the jobs run, they check the crucible server set as primary in settings and use that one"). Paused holds work. The Mac and owens-pc are both registered; the Mac is primary. Readiness has five states and one door.
- **P2.** One transport (`electron/crucible/transport.ts`): chats stream, `finish_reason` must be real, a truncated stream is a hard failure, over-context throws before sending and the middle-truncation is gone. `claude-cli:` is untouched and outside Crucible (#193). The Anthropic/OpenAI/Ollama clients, the init pings, `PROVIDER_DEFAULTS` and the AI setup wizard are deleted. `api-keys.json` migrates once into the primary server's settings and the app holds no key. The routing dialog lists only what the primary server offers.
- **P3.** One GPU lane per server; cloud and claude -p calls take no lane. A busy or leased card PARKS the item with the holder's sentence and re-admits it when the card clears; nothing moves to another server on its own (#205); the fast pin is the only route to the PC. The in-flight ledger is written before anything else; startup and quit sweeps release only what we hold. Stall watchdogs (10 min of silence) replace the wall-clock caps. One job per server, Mac and PC in parallel. The inputs page shows parked rows, a Fast toggle and a lanes strip.
- **P5.** Transcription is Crucible's `asr` on `qwen3-asr-1.7b` with the aligner, pipeline and editor (#206). The context carries the filler instruction and the item's facts. Measured: the seed spells names right (Hank Kunneman 6/6 vs 4/6) but pulled "Gestapo"→"stopper" and "going to"→"gonna" ×3 (open: names-only context); fillers 9–11 per the reference window against 19 direct, set by Crucible's cut points, not the context; Qwen's word starts sit 0.3 s after whisper.cpp's, and against the audio's own onsets whisper.cpp is ~0.45 s early while Qwen is within ~0.1 s — §8.3's gate is restated against the audio, and the editor runs on Qwen.
- **P7.** Voice isolation is the `denoise` job on `vocals-roformer`; Python keeps chunking and reassembly. Its live null test and listening clip are still owed (the card was held all session).
- **P8a.** The chaptering service (`electron/services/metadata/chaptering/`, #199) matches the Python reference on 43 Viterbi matrices, 24 ad-confirm cases and the sentence splitter, exactly. Live on the Mac: the 2026-09-23 stream at `stories` found 5 of 7 of Owen's own edit edges within 60 s and his 1:53:15–2:16:08 cut to the second. Titles run with thinking OFF (on, 4 of 9 blew the budget at up to 412 s each). `episodes` needs a stream-level outline (P8b).
- **Stories/tags.** The editor Stories analyzer follows the chapters routing; `nomic-embed-text` is gone and tag pools come from the chapter list (#205).

**208. Chapters and stories are the two grains, and thinking is on where the work might need it (Owen, 2026-09-25).**
- **Two names.** "chapters are what makes it to youtube - subject changes in videos. stories are stream-level splits that are completely different subjects, not just small changes within the same subjects." The chaptering service serves exactly these two grains: `chapters` (the metadata pipeline) and `stories` (the editor's Stories and the in-queue split of a stream into standalone videos — "episodes" is retired as a name). "in theory, we should be able to draw chapters out at any granularity level": one service, the outline instructions and the switch cost are the dial.
- **Stream-level stories** come from a full outline built from the chunk outlines and merged into one large outline, then one pass over the whole stream (Owen: "i think we're on the same page with this").
- **Thinking.** Every call already states `thinking` per call through the transport (`chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking`, P2), and the budget is already a setting. Owen: "i would prefer to turn thinking on if we're doing something that might require it." So a judgment call (chapter and story titles and summaries, the description) runs thinking ON with a declared budget; a mechanical one (the outline list, more-titles, tags) runs it off. The 2026-09-25 title timings with thinking on (37–412 s each on the Mac's 27B, 4 of 9 over an 8,192 budget) are the cost to measure against a larger budget, not a reason to turn it off.
- **Ads sit at about 5:00 and 10:00** in Owen's videos. That is a prior for the ad check, never a substitute for detecting them.

**209. Load only the context a job needs (Owen, 2026-09-25: "we should only be using as much context (8k vs 16k) as necessary. if we need the 16k then that's fine, keep it. but if we don't need 16k then reduce it").** The load context is per JOB: the smallest step (8,192 → 16,384 → 32,768 …) that fits the job's largest call, prompt plus answer budget, by the transport's own arithmetic (plan §7.2). A model resident at a larger context than the next job needs is reloaded at the smaller one, and the reload is logged as a declared cost. The old rule — grow the resident model's context, never shrink it — is dead. The mlx batch width itself stays at full (Owen, same day: "i want to use mlx to its fullest potential"); what is bounded is the context each job asks for, and the app runs one job on the card at a time. P4 measures the real prompt sizes so that 8k is the common case (the digest) and 16k is asked for only by the calls that need it (thinking-on titles and the scrub on the 27B).

**210. The re-roll gate is built, wired and measured on the rules; it ships OFF until its calibration finishes (2026-09-25).** #201 as code: every title, chapter title, description sentence (hook included), thumbnail text and pinned comment is asked its field's rules as snap yes/no statements that QUOTE it, on a fixed declared scorer (`REROLL_SCORER_MODEL`, the 9B); what fails goes back, per unit, to its field's routed model with the failed rule named in positive form, at most 3 rounds, and each unit keeps its best-scoring attempt, flagged in the warnings when it still fails. Titles are ranked by a choice asked once per rotation (position bias cancels), reported as a share of an even split. Measured against 70 hand labels written before any model read them: the gate's verdict agrees on 61 of 62 (22/22 violators caught, 1 false alarm). The §0a baseline trap was real (the first "narrates" wording put P(yes) 0.73 on clean titles) and was fixed in the statement, not with a baseline. `sentence` (chapter titles as sentences) is measured but not gating: Owen's own edits are sentences. Stopped on the coordinator's instruction (one thing on the card at a time): the A/B ranking has 4 of 177 tests and the corpus distributions are unrun. [docs/crucible/P9.md; electron/services/metadata/reroll/; PROMPT-LEARNINGS Part 12]

**211. The gate judges by Owen's standard, and the generation prompts are not touched (Owen, 2026-09-25).** On the re-roll gate's questions: chapter titles are "one or two sentences describing what's being discussed. how youtube expects them to appear in the description"; "X, refuted" / "X, mocked line by line" is a fine format; descriptions may carry calls to action. On all three: "they're coming out fine as is. don't change the prompts." So the gate's rule statements and hand labels move to match — a call to action is not a reference to the creator, a ", refuted" title is not narration, and the sentence rule never gates — and no generation prompt changes on the gate's account. The ranking threshold for showing the rank by default is decided when the A/B run is in: "we're going to have to see when we get there."

**212. Stories are found by 45-second windows judged one against the last, on snap; chapters keep outline + assign (Owen, 2026-09-25).** "we might not be able to perfect livestream stories. that's ok. if it can approximate where they start and end then i can adjust them as needed. although we got REALLY close to perfect when we were taking it in 45 second chunks with a smaller model. we could probably try that again with snap - send 45 seconds worth of text through and judge whether it's a new subject from the one before. i think chapters are good as is, building an outline and asking where it fits, but for stories, the 45 second resolution trick we used before might be the better choice." The stream-level merged outline over-coarsened (2 of 7 of Owen's edges within 60 s, the Pokémon story lost), so it is dropped. The stories grain is the editor analyzer's old shape (`chapter-splitter.ts`: 45 s stretches, a rating of each junction against what came before), re-done as one snap yes/no per junction quoting the previous stretch and the new one, with the probability map over all junctions and a minimum story length placing the cuts; Owen adjusts the rest by hand. Outline + assign stays for chapters, unchanged. `chapter-splitter.ts` is kept as the reference until the snap version matches it on the 2026-09-23 stream.

**213. The inputs page offers "stories" or "chapters", and the choice follows what the item IS (Owen, 2026-09-25).** The three-way "Chapters detect" selector (detailed / broad / stories, #170) collapses to two: `chapters` (default) or `stories`. "we'll generate stories for cases where i send a podcast episode through. it's a compilation of the week's videos and it goes to patreon and spreaker. and for everything else, we'll use chapters. they'll be single videos (sometimes an hour long for unfiltered, but they will always be 1 'story')." So a weekly podcast compilation is chaptered at the stories grain (#212's 45-second junction method) and every single video at the chapters grain (outline + assign), however long it is. **And in the editor, splitting a full master livestream is always stories, not chapters** (Owen, same night). Stored `detailed`/`broad` values migrate to `chapters` with a logged line; the queue row and the job record carry the new value.

### Doc hygiene (staleness is itself a recorded state — see Part I §4 for the worklist)

**97.** `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` / `README.md` / `QUICKSTART.md` / `SETUP_STATUS.md`: unmodified since 2025-10-30, describe the pre-Angular "LaunchPad" app; declared non-authoritative; the rewrite is an unfinished Phase 0b task.
**98.** `BUNDLING.md` / `ASAR-LAYOUT.md` / `SCRIPTS-SUMMARY.md` / `scripts/README.md`: describe a Python-venv bundling world, reference five deleted scripts; stale and NOT flagged anywhere.
**99.** `extension/README.md` says the collector is an unbuilt scaffold with Bearer-token auth; both false since 2026-07-22.
**100.** `AB-TEST-PLAN.md` §4/§8 still calls the A/B loop "never written"; superseded by PR #13, correction lives only in TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md.
**101.** `TITLE-EVIDENCE-PLAN.md`'s first draft targeted the WRONG REPO (a year-old Python app at ~/Projects/ContentStudio) — discarded on the record, along with per-variant CTR as a goal (YouTube decides on watch time; `impressionsCtrPct` is video-level).
**102.** Intake discipline recorded: "taking another agent's file into the repo unread is how a stale premise becomes a repo fact."

**214. Metadata never gets cheaped out (Owen, 2026-09-26).** Answers to P8b's open questions. (1) Thinking titles keep a generous budget: the 16,384 budget stands unless a measurement shows it is never needed (P8b measured 572–10,041 output tokens per title; 4 of 9 blew 8,192). Speed does not justify lowering it: "if we dont need the whole 16k budget we can lower it but i dont want to cheap out on metadata. its important." (2) The PC will probably never run titles, but the Crucible agent is asked to load the PC's 27B with a larger context so thinking titles are not refused there if ever needed. *Answered the same night:* the PC's qwen3.8-27b-4bit manifest already allows `max_context` 32,768 (the most that fits on the 3090 Ti's KV pool; 16,384 is only the default when a load asks for nothing), so no server change is needed: the caller passes `params.context` on load-model, which the app already does per call (`loadContextFor`, #209; a thinking title asks for 24,576). Computed, not yet load-tested on the card; one full-length request fits at a time. (3) The 5:00/10:00 ad prior stays as built (not overruled). (4) No Settings control that trades metadata quality for speed: "dont cheap out on metadata." Title thinking stays on; the chapter engine stays a store key and CLI flag, not a user-facing knob.

**215. P8c shipped on branch `p8c-stories`: stories by 45-second junctions, the chapters | stories pick (2026-09-26).** What was built and measured, no new decision. #212 as code: `chaptering/stories.ts` cuts ~45 s stretches at sentence ends, asks one snap yes/no per junction quoting both stretches ("a completely different story"), ranks by P(yes) with the reference's duration cadence and minimum gap (33 cuts, 216 s on a 3.4 h stream), places each cut by a choice over the window's lines, and consolidates adjacent pairs most-probable-first while the 9B says one story; the merged stream outline is deleted and chapters are unchanged. Measured on the 2026-09-23 stream against Owen's 7 edges (≤60 s): 3/7 (the cut start to the second, the Flashpoint return at 9 s, the empathy start at 46 s), in 794 s; the first junction wording scored 2/7, and a two-stretch-only state 2/7. Misses: the u1 start is a speaker change, the u2/u3 seam (two chapters of one book) was found and then merged, the cut end fell inside the minimum gap. #213 as code: the inputs selector is chapters (default) | stories, detailed/broad migrate to chapters with one logged line, chapters always run at 20, `story:analyze-chapters` takes no grain and always splits into stories (a story's own chapters moved to `story:chapter-story`). Duffy three-way: snap thinking-off 7 chapters in 214 s (10,038 / 629 chat tokens); whole-transcript 7 chapters in 1,111 s (22,591 / 21,312); snap thinking-on did not finish, twice: the Mac 27B engine died with `metal::malloc Resource limit (499000) exceeded` on title 2 and the call hung past the stall clock. [docs/crucible/P8c.md]

**216. The Mac 27B's thinking-title crash was Crucible's, found from the app's exact bytes (2026-09-26).** P8c's snap thinking-on Duffy run died twice on chapter title 2 (`metal::malloc Resource limit (499000) exceeded`), and the call then hung. Reproduced deterministically with the real request bodies (captured by the new `CONTENTSTUDIO_DUMP_CHAT_BODIES`, ae51774; a rebuild from the prompt code was 24% short because the run uses the speaker-tagged title prompt). The Crucible agent bisected it on a bare engine: mlx-lm 0.31.3's batched caches keep lazy, never-evaluated bookkeeping arrays, the prompt cache stores that graph with each reply, and on Qwen3.5/3.8 the next request's first step evaluates it all and passes Metal's buffer-count limit. Two Crucible fixes, not yet deployed on the Mac: 42a1731 (evaluate the counters in the decode step) and f8ae4f6 (a dead engine thread exits the process, so the in-flight call fails as `engine_unreachable` and later ones as `502 engine_exited`). Until deployed, every thinking-on title on the Mac 27B crashes the engine on its second call; there is no client-side workaround. The app changes nothing for it: P8c question 5 (a tokens-only stall clock) is withdrawn, and the snap thinking-on Duffy run is owed after the deploy.

**217. P4 built: one sizing rule, answer-sized budgets, the digest as a declared policy, the capability question before a load (2026-09-26; checks and fake only, no live run).** What was built and measured, no new ruling on Owen's behalf. (1) #209 as one rule: every local call asks for the smallest 8,192 step that holds its own prompt, its own budget and 512 by the door's estimate (`context-check.ts loadContextFor`); the per-model run window, the 4096-bucket sizer, the job's context ratchet and the Settings `chapterNumCtx` 16,384 floor are gone (the lease already grows a load once and never shrinks it within a job); the re-roll scorer's decide, which stated no load at all, now states its step. (2) Budgets by #214's rule (lower only on evidence, ≥2× the largest answer): the thinking-off field calls (titles, thumbnail, pinned, tags, more titles) 8,192 → 2,048 (largest of 238 stored answers 1,369 chars, ~392 tokens; Crucible-era 49–121 out); the scrub and Soften, thinking-on, 8,192 → 16,384 (a recorded chapter-titles scrub spent 6,602); the description stays 4,096 (one stored 6,066-char run-on, ~1,733 tokens, needs 3,466 at 2×); the summarizer, package, distiller and re-roll revise keep theirs for want of evidence. (3) The field input policy (plan 7.2): `raw` (today's rule, the declared default) or `digest` (every chaptered item's fields read `renderChapterDigest`; a chapterless item keeps its transcript; chapterless over the ceiling still fails naming both facts), a store key `fieldInput` and the CLI's `--field-input`, never a Settings control; each item records `_field_input` and a logged 16,384 assertion over its own trace (`_context_stats`), never a block. (4) Offline, with the app's own builders: on three 74–78 minute videos the digest puts every field call at ≤ ~9k prompt tokens → 16,384; raw needs 32,768 at the new budgets (40,960 at the old); on Duffy and the Kofi Asare / Tasia Fortune / Forgiato Blow video the digest puts every field call at 8,192. The thinking-on calls (snap titles, the scrub) need 24,576 by their budgets, inside the PC 27B's 32,768. The name scaffold is already windowed (2,800 words, ~4.6k tokens) and the compilation chunk is ~17.7k tokens → 24,576; both fit the Mac 27B's 131,072 (and the PC's 32,768), so neither changed; the distiller's prompt is ~2.1k. (5) The SDK exposes `capability?class=generate&context_tokens=&concurrency=`; lease.ts asks it before every load at the load's size and concurrency 1, and a no-fit fails `over_context` with the server's sentence before anything is loaded or evicted (at the load rather than queue admission, because no prompt exists at admission; a failure rather than a park, because waiting does not change a host ceiling). Owen still decides: the digest default (plan 7.4's blind A/B); the identical description candidates (mlx-lm decodes at its default temperature 0.0 when none is sent, so the three local candidates come out the same text: a local temperature, or one candidate); whether #209's cross-job shrink-reload is built (on the Mac the load context is admission only, so a shrink buys nothing there); the description budget's one outlier. [docs/crucible/P4.md]

**218. P10 shipped on its branch: Ollama, whisper.cpp, voice-separator-env and the openai / @anthropic-ai/sdk packages are out of the app, and existing installs lose the retired files once (2026-09-26).** What was built, no new decision. Removed: whisper-bridge.ts and the runtime-paths whisper exports; the `whisper-engine`/`whisper-*` components, the `whisperModel` setting and its Settings and setup-dialog pickers; the editor's `whisper-large-v3-turbo` asset (it was still `required`, so a clean editor install downloaded 1.6 GB), `whisper-base` and `voice-separator-env` assets, `getWhisperCliPath`/`getWhisperModelPath`, the `editor-backend/utilities/bin` extraResource (whisper-cli shipped in every package), `download-whisper-cpp.js` and `download-all.sh`, transcribe.py's whisper-cli JSON reader; setup.sh's Ollama steps and the `ollama` keyword; `openai` and `@anthropic-ai/sdk` from package.json and the lock (nothing imported them since P2; `--claude-cli` already spawned `claude -p` on the transport seam). Renamed: `WhisperService` → `TranscriptionService` (transcription.service.ts); a two-line alias stays until the two P4-owned importers are edited (docs/crucible/P10.md §6). Voice-isolation stems are read as 16-bit PCM WAV only and anything else is refused by name: no LEDGER entry recorded the change, so it was checked in the Mac's installed Crucible 1.0.40 source (denoise `OUTPUT_FORMAT = "WAV"`, a mismatched container fails the job, audio-separator writes int16). The cleanup (`electron/retired-components.ts`) runs 15 s after boot, logs its whole plan with sizes before removing, asserts every path inside its parent and inside userData or the OwenMorgan shared dir and clear of what the build uses (at plan time and again before each removal), records `retiredComponents.p10-crucible` and runs once; `keepRetiredComponents: true` keeps everything. Its read-only dry run on the Mac lists 3.86 GB in 5 items: `components/whisper-engine` and `whisper-base`, the shared dir's `ggml-base.bin` and `ggml-large-v3-turbo.bin`, and `runtime/voice-separator-env`. Those shared-dir slots came from the asset manager inherited from AutoCutStudio, whose app would find them gone if run again. `npm run check:legacy-gone` (the §15 grep test, plus both download catalogs as built) and `npm run check:retired` (8) pass, with every existing check. [docs/crucible/P10.md]

**219. A thumbnail outside YouTube's bounds is FITTED, never refused (Owen, 2026-09-26: "if a youtube thumbnail is too small or too big, i.e. its outside of the bounds youtube sets, it's automatically resized to be within the bounds. under 2 mb, a certain resolution, whatever").** Supersedes #27's "validators never fix" for thumbnails (audio validation is unchanged). The bounds are YouTube's own: at most 2 MiB; at least 640x360; and the 1280x720 frame YouTube stores a thumbnail at, taken as the maximum because pixels above it never reach a viewer. A real PNG or JPEG outside any of them gets a copy written BESIDE it as `<stem> (2).png`, scaled to keep its shape (too big shrinks into the frame, too small grows to clear the floor), PNG first and JPEG (quality 95 → 80) only when the PNG is still over the byte limit, and the COPY is what is attached and uploaded; the original is never touched. The shape is never changed: an image that cannot clear the floor without breaking the frame (a portrait, a very wide strip) is refused naming both numbers, because the fix is a crop and a crop is the operator's. What is NOT an image (missing, empty, wrong magic bytes, an extension that lies) is refused as before. `fitThumbnailFile` is the one door for every path that attaches or hands bytes to YouTube (hand pick, proposal, automatic pass, rescan, carry-forward, upload, push, the extension bridge), so a master replaced by a larger export after it was attached is fitted again at upload; previews only measure. The non-16:9 and under-recommended warnings stay warnings. Checked by `npm run check:thumbnail` (the arithmetic in plain Node, the real copies under the electron binary). [thumbnail-validate.ts; tools/thumbnail-checks.js; tools/thumbnail-fit-smoke.js]

**220. Owen's rulings on P4's open decisions (2026-09-26).** (1) **One description, one call.** "i would rather make a single call to get a single description and do it right the first time if possible." `DESCRIPTION_CANDIDATES` is 1 (description-unit.ts); `description_options` is no longer produced, and the two extra calls per video are gone. The alternative, a temperature on local candidate draws so the 27B's three copies would differ, was open to him ("im fine with using temperature") and is not needed once there is one draw; the transport already carries `temperature` when a call sets it, so nothing is asked of Crucible. (2) **Shrink across jobs, everywhere.** #209's second sentence is now built in lease.ts: a model left resident by an earlier job at a larger context than the next job asks for is reloaded at the smaller one, on the Mac as on the PC ("it would be good to have the ability to. we should probably build it everywhere"). On the Mac this is a reload that frees no memory (mlx allocates KV on demand) and it is logged as a declared cost; another client's lease is never disturbed by it (the job runs under theirs and says so). Within one job the window still only grows. (3) **Descriptions are short.** "descriptions dont need to be long. we hit major keywords. one paragraph preferably, plus the one line explainer that appears in searches." The contract already is one paragraph opening on the hook sentence (the search snippet); the 6,066-character run-on P4 had kept the 4,096 budget for is a malfunction, not an answer, so the description budget is 2,048. (4) The digest default (plan §7.4) is explained to Owen in plain words and awaits his answer; the remaining P8c questions he will meet in his own testing. [description-unit.ts; lease.ts; tools/test-crucible-shrink.js; tools/test-crucible-p4.js]

**221. The setup gate is hard: a Crucible server must be connected and ready before the app can be used (Owen, 2026-09-26: "there is no 'set up later'. it MUST have a crucible installed and set up before the user can use the app").** The first-run dialog no longer has a close button or a "Set up later" action, and it no longer points at Settings: it IS the Settings › Crucible Servers pane, shown modally, so it lists the Crucible found on this computer (adopt, start, or install it) and adds one on another computer by pairing or connect code. It opens the moment startup readiness is anything but ready, including main's provisional "Checking Crucible on …" answer (which is what Owen saw: the dialog had read the snapshot before the first probe returned and never refreshed), asks for a real derivation, and closes by itself on the readiness push that says ready. Once the app has been ready in a session, a later outage is the readiness banner's to report, not the gate's, so a network blip cannot lock the operator out of work on screen. [environment-setup.ts; environment-setup-dialog.*]

**222. The Crucible server is picked in Model routing (Owen, 2026-09-26: "we can pick which crucible server we use (wsl or mac) in model routing").** How it is built, no further ruling on Owen's behalf. The routing stores `metadataRouting.server`, a registered server's name, beside the field selections. A save naming a server the registry does not have is refused with the sentence naming it. A stored server that was forgotten from the registry drops back to unset: one logged line, and the store is rewritten without it. Unset means the Settings-selected server, so a routing saved before the key changes nothing. The venue rule lives in one place, venue-decision.ts `intendedVenue`: the fast pin → fastServer (#195 still wins for a pinned item); else the routing's server; else the selected one. lanes.ts passes the routing's server in, reading it once per admission. A paused or unreachable routing server parks the job with the existing sentences, and nothing moves (#205). The admission line's third `because` is "the routing's server", and `describeRouting` names the server. A job never spans servers. Its transcription follows the job venue (asr-venue.ts, unchanged). Its chapters, scrub, gate and field calls run on its lane. Its upstream (cloud) calls go to the routing server it was admitted with, else the selected one (`upstreamServerFor`); with no routing server, that is plan §0 #20 as before. Not metadata jobs, so unchanged: the editor's transcription, the episode splitter, and standalone calls outside a job (story title, the reports page's more titles and soften stay on the selected server). The metadata CLI's `--server` is the CLI's spelling of the routing's server for that run, and the CLI goes through the same `intendedVenue`. The routing dialog has a "Runs on" row: "The selected server (<name>)" or a registered server with its reach word. Its options are judged against the server the jobs would run on. Settings › Crucible Servers stays the registry; when a routing server is set, the pane says in one sentence that it overrides Select for metadata jobs. Checked by `tools/test-crucible-routing-server.js` (fake Crucible, 7 checks) and five pure checks in `check:pure`; no live run. [venue-decision.ts; metadata-routing.ts; docs/crucible/routing-server.md]

**223. A scrub failure never discards the item, and every model-written section gets a re-roll button (Owen, 2026-09-26).**

(1) **The cleanup is applied field by field.** This supersedes #183's "failure is the item's failure", and #184's "failure writes nothing" for the button. The incident: a 60-minute run finished every field. The scrub then got 27 lines back for 26 chapter titles, threw, and the whole item was failed, with 20+ minutes of output lost.

- Each plan is applied or not on its own: the opening line, the description, each alternate, the chapter titles. A plan whose call or read fails, or that cannot even be planned, leaves its field exactly as generated. The others are applied.
- Nothing of a failed plan is applied. A miscounted list is never matched back partially, and nothing re-asks.
- The item is saved as usual with `scrubbed.failed`: field, item key, a plain reason ("Chapter titles were not cleaned up: the model returned 27 lines for 26 titles.") and the full error, numbered Sent/Returned included.
- Each failure is a warn line, and in a run also a run warning (Laws 3 and 8). A cancel still stops the pass.
- The count error is typed, `RewriteShapeError` (Law 10). Soften still writes nothing when one field fails.
- The reports page shows each failure beside its section, with **Clean up again**. It re-runs just the failed parts (`onlyKeys`). The older receipt's failures for the other sections are carried forward, so a clean re-run clears only its own warning.

(2) **Re-roll buttons.** Description (opening line and body), chapter titles and summaries, thumbnail text and pinned comment each get **Re-roll** and **Put back**. "10 more titles" is unchanged. Tags and hashtags have no button because code builds them.

- A re-roll replays the run's own stored prompt from `_prompt_trace` (#179's rule), on the field's currently routed model (#204); there is no picker.
- Two things change before sending. The recorded system turn is removed. On chapters, each call's two context lines are re-pointed at the titles just written, by the run's own `contextLines`.
- Chapters keep their boundaries and are titled on the chapters row, thinking on (#208).
- The answer replaces the section in place. The cleanup then runs over the new description or chapter titles, as in a run.
- After chapters, the tags and hashtags are rebuilt by the run's own assembly: `codeOwnedTagFields` (now shared with metadata-tasks) and `finalizeTagFields`. They are checked against the item's saved transcript or linked story (`finished-item-content.ts`). When that text cannot be read, they are left as they were, and the result says so.

**Previous versions** go on the item as `reroll_history.<section>[]`. **Put back** swaps the newest kept version with the current one, so no version is ever lost. The .txt and the publish record are untouched.

- One rewrite at a time per item (re-roll, put-back or cleanup), refused by name.
- The write is queued and refused if the section changed while the calls were out.
- A failed re-roll writes nothing and shows its error inline. The exception is chapters: an unreadable chapter keeps its title and is named. If no chapter came back usable, nothing is written.
- A hand-edited section asks for a second press (an inline armed button; no browser dialogs).

Pools sizes moved to tags-hashtags.ts. Checked by `tools/scrub-reroll-checks.js` (12 checks, in `check:pure`); nothing was run live. The open points are in docs/crucible/reroll-buttons.md HANDOFF. **224. The setup gate opens only when there is no Crucible at all (Owen, 2026-09-26: "every time i open the app it pops up the crucible server connection screen ... it shouldnt pop it up unless theres no crucible server installed").** #221 opened the gate on the startup snapshot before the first probe returned, so every launch showed it until the server answered. It now opens only on a derived `not-installed` or `not-configured` answer, which always names its repair (`install` / `connect`). A server that exists but has not answered yet (`starting`, `unreachable`, main's provisional "Checking Crucible on …" or "Checking for Crucible...") leaves it shut; the readiness banner reports those. Built without a live launch. [environment-setup.ts]

**225. A running queue row always shows it is working, and the queue never forgets a job main is running (Owen, 2026-09-26: "it just sat there doing nothing ... give me a visual indication its doing something").** Main was working the whole time in both of his sessions and sent a progress event every ~17 s; the page showed none of it. Causes, all fixed: (1) the row never displayed the event's words — `currentlyProcessing` was written but no template read it, a 1 s timer overwrote it with "Processing... (Ns)", and the item said only "Transcribed" or "Generating..." with a bar that moved 50 → 54 % in four minutes; (2) a reused saved transcript left the item on "Transcribed" through the lessons and chapter work; (3) a window reload (⌘R reloads the page in the packaged app) restored the queue from sessionStorage with the running row set back to pending, a leftover "Transcribed" item, no listener for main's events, and a Start button that could not start it (the lanes plan skips a job it is running) — the screen Owen saw at 19:02, clock icon, Fast, X, "Start Queue (1 job)"; (4) the queue's run state and 1 s tick lived on the Inputs page, which is destroyed when another tab is opened, so a re-opened page could hide Start while nothing ticked. Now: a running row has a spinner, its stage in plain words and a clock that ticks every second from Start to the end ("Finding chapters · sorting sentences 128 of 469 · 3:12"), a labelled Stop button, and the item row repeats the stage; the words come from one pure module, frontend `job-activity.ts` (`stageLine`, `itemAfter`, `elapsedClock`, `parkedText`, `laneBusyText`), an unknown event shown as main wrote it. A reused saved transcript moves the item straight to generating. Main sends a `lessons` event ("Preparing channel lessons") before the guidelines distillation, the ~20 s claude -p call that had no event. The run (started, target) and the plan guard live in JobQueueService and the run is kept in sessionStorage; a page made again takes the tick back. A reloaded window asks main `metadata:running-jobs` and shows those rows running again, following them to their end; `generate-metadata` refuses a job id that is already running, by name. While the run is on or any job runs, the Start button's place says "The queue is running: 1 job working …". A parked row reads "Waiting for <server> — <why>"; a row held behind another job of the queue says it starts when its server is free. "Waiting" on a running job was the readiness sentence "busy: contentstudio, asr 76% done; AI work waits its turn", said about ContentStudio's own job: it now says "busy with ContentStudio's own work: transcription 76% done" (readiness.ts `readyReason`), and a lane chip says "Busy with other ContentStudio work (…)" for a card held by work this queue did not start. The second job in the log (created 18:44:06, cancelled 18:44:20 without starting) was Owen queueing the video again because the first looked dead and then removing it; the X on a pending row asks main to cancel, which is the log line. 'mute words' … harsher words … mute all bad words in the first 3 minutes").** A **Mute words** modal, opened from the processing modal (button + one-line summary) and from a project's right-click menu, picks per word group (F-word, Harsh words, General swearing, Your words) Off / Everywhere / First m:ss only, plus "Mute all swearing in the first [3] minutes" (every group, Off ones included, inside the window). The groups live in ONE data file, `editor-backend/core/mute_words.json`, which the modal shows and the pass matches with. The choice is saved per project (`<cleanName>_mute-words.json` in the project folder), and the last one saved is remembered for new projects; Process keeps the remembered one for a project that has none. **Order:** the mutes are written at EXPORT, as the last edit to the tree (after cuts, stories, reorder, mic mute), because the word times do not exist when processing writes the zip and only the finished timeline knows which clip pieces survive and where 0:00 is; "Save and apply" re-mutes an export already on disk (`cli/mute_words.py`, repeatable: earlier mutes are removed first). **Placement:** a word's aligned file span (Qwen3 aligner), padded `MUTE_PAD_SECONDS` = 50 ms each side (Owen's to tune), maps to the timeline through the file's segment table (ManifestBuilder leaves), then to each carrying clip's source time through the pristine master spine, then onto every exported clip piece whose source range holds it: a word across a cut is muted on both sides, a word in a removed section writes nothing and is listed. The master's audio is on `<ref-clip>`s (lane -1 CAM = mics, lane -2 SSB = screen), so the mute is `<audio-role-source role="dialogue.dialogue-1"><mute start="N/720000s" duration="M/720000s"/>` (FCPXML 1.14 DTD position, ref-clip source time); a `<clip>` in Owen's sample shape gets his exact `<audio-channel-source srcCh="1, 2" …><mute …/>`, rebuilt byte-for-structure by the check. The opening window is judged on master-timeline time (after cuts; each story from its own start). **234. ContentStudio checks Crucible on a timer only while work is queued, and a job waiting behind another app's work shows that work at the bottom of the queue (Owen, 2026-09-26: "i dont think we need it polling crucible unless its waiting", then "it wont poll unless something is in the queue. if nothing is in the queue, it doesnt poll. if there are items in the queue, it polls"; and "lets give content studio some way to report that its waiting its turn if it genuinely is queued and waiting ... itll show the job that's currently running on crucible and how long it has until it's done at the bottom of the queue ... if something else takes the lease first, it fills in that slot with the new running job").** Two timers ran forever. Readiness re-derived every 30 s (10 s when not ready), and the lanes' preflight read every server's `/v1/activity` every 15 s. So an idle app kept probing and logged another app's job progress.

A transcription started after processing is not hidden by the session's opening read (Owen, 2026-09-28: "i didnt know it was running. then that means we need to give the user an indication").** Processing with "transcribe when finished" opens the session and starts the job at once. The bootstrap reads the transcript sidecar WITHOUT awaiting it, so that read usually landed after `startTranscription` had set the pane to 'running'; finding no sidecar yet it set 'none', which put the Transcribe button back over a live Crucible job and hid the activity dock's transcription row, and `onTranscribeProgress` then dropped every event because the state was no longer 'running'. Found on the 2026-09-27 session: job e617ab4e at 63% on the Mac while the pane offered Transcribe. The opening read now stands aside when a transcription is running (`loadTranscriptForSession(..., { initial: true })`); the job's completion reloads the sidecar as before. A press of Transcribe in that state would have started a second job on the card. Untested by Owen. [editor.component.ts]

**236. A mute on a compound clip names the main role (Owen, 2026-09-28: "i expected the system to mute swears but it didnt").** The 2026-09-27 export carried 78 mutes for 73 words, all inside `<audio-role-source role="dialogue.dialogue-1">` on the master timeline's audio-only DC CAM / DC SSB ref-clips — the same clips the mic mute switches off, so the placement was right — and Final Cut imported them and applied none. The file validates against the FCPXML 1.13 and 1.14 DTDs, so nothing refused it; Final Cut simply ignores a ref-clip mute under the subrole. Two variants of that export were imported by Owen: **A**, the same file with `role="dialogue"`, and **B**, each word's piece bladed and switched off as the mic mute does. Both went silent at the words he checked (Noah's Ark, Alex Jones on AI). **A was taken**: it is the real R+V mute, one element per word, with no extra cuts on the timeline. `word_mute_pass.write_mutes` now names the MAIN role on a ref-clip (`main_role`, one container per main role, spans of sibling subroles merged); a `<clip>` keeps the full subrole, as in Owen's own sample. His 2026-09-27 export was re-applied in place (`cli/mute_words.py`): 78 mutes replaced, the role the only byte that changed. check:mutes 50/50. [cli/word_mute_pass.py, tools/word-mute-driver.py]

It reads the OPEN session's project choice in the processing screen's words (`Mute words: Harsh words everywhere; all swearing in the first 3:00`), lit when anything is on and grey `Mute words: Off` when nothing is saved or nothing is chosen; a long choice is cut short and read whole on hover. A click opens the same Mute words modal the project right-click opens, on this session's project (folder and cleanName from the zip's own name, the zip, and whether the transcript is ready), so Save keeps the choice and "Save and apply" re-mutes the export already on disk. The pill re-reads after either modal closes and on every session load; a session outside a project folder shows `Mute words: n/a`, disabled, with the reason; a choice that cannot be read shows `Mute words: error` with the message. **238. Stray ums: a lone um or uh in a piece of its own is cut whole (Owen, 2026-09-28: "any time there's an 'um' or an 'uh' as its own standalone word in the mic1 audio, and it has cuts on either side, remove the whole cut ... if it's connected to something already, if it doesnt have cuts on both sides, if there are more words in its connected group, leave it").** The rule is a pure module, `model/stray-fillers.ts`: a piece of a mic track (the mic-mute pass's test: "mic" in label or file, not "screen") qualifies when (1) the ONLY transcript word touching it, on any track including the screen audio, is one filler on that mic (`^(u+m+|u+h+m*)$` after case and punctuation are dropped: um, umm, uh, uhh, uhm; not uh-huh, hmm, umbrella), and (2) BOTH its edges are cuts, meaning the neighbouring piece is not contiguous on the timeline and in the file. The first and last pieces are left, since they have no cut on one side; a piece with no words is not inferred to be anything; a piece already cut is not offered again. It is a top-bar button, not automatic: `Stray ums: N`, lit while N > 0, recounted on transcript load and on every cut change so N is exactly what a click removes; one click adds every piece as a cut in ONE undo step. Dry run on the 2026-09-27 session: 33 mic fillers, 17 stray (0.7 to 2.2 s each). `npm run check:strays` 15/15; frontend build clean; untested by Owen. [model/stray-fillers.ts, editor.component.ts/.html, tools/stray-filler-checks.js, package.json]

**239. A mute never covers the next word (Owen, 2026-09-28: "can we make the mute a little bit shorter on swears? it's cutting off the next word slightly. it would be preferable to have part of the harsh word unmuted than the next word muted").** #226 padded the aligned word by 50 ms on BOTH sides. Now `core.word_mutes.mute_span`: 50 ms LEAD before the word (the hard "f" stays covered) but never back into the previous word on that track; the end is TRIMMED 60 ms (at most a third of the word) instead of padded, and never past the next word's start on that track, even when the aligner overlaps the two. A swear the neighbours leave nothing of is reported NOT MUTED (`overlaps-next`) rather than muting them. Neighbours that are themselves matched swears are not avoided but bridged (gap ≤ `MUTE_BRIDGE_SECONDS` 250 ms), so "fuck, fucking" is one mute with no sliver between. Re-applied to Owen's 2026-09-27 export: 73/73 still muted, 78 mutes, average length 0.515 s → 0.363 s, total 40.2 s → 28.3 s — more than the trim alone accounts for, i.e. many mutes had been running into the next word. check:mutes 55/55. [core/word_mutes.py, cli/word_mute_pass.py, tools/word-mute-driver.py]

- **The rule** is one function, readiness.ts `needsPolling`: poll while a window's queue holds an unfinished job (pending, running, parked or held), while main's lanes hold a job (running, parked or in line), or while a start or install of the local Crucible runs.
- **Queue count.** The renderer's JobQueueService reports its count on `crucible:queue-count` whenever it changes. Main keeps the count per window and forgets a window when it closes, so the editor window's empty queue never cancels the main window's. The lanes' own count covers a window that lost its list.
- **What follows the rule.** Readiness switches both timers (`onPolling` → lanes `setPolling`); nothing else starts them. Intervals are unchanged. The lease heartbeat, event streams, the transcription upload ticker, the stall clock and the sweeps belong to running work and are not governed.
- **Otherwise on demand:**
  - once at app start;
  - on a registry or selection change;
  - when the Servers pane opens (it now derives rather than reads the stored answer) and on Re-check or Test;
  - when the queue page opens (the lanes are read once, only when not polling);
  - right before a job is admitted or a standalone call runs (lanes `beforeAdmit` → readiness `freshCheck`). An answer older than `FRESH_PROBE_MS` (5 s) is checked again, and the venue's reach at admission uses the same 5 s limit instead of the 15 s cache. A CLI, which never starts the loops, takes no extra probe.
- **Freshness is never claimed.** The view carries `polling`. When the view is not polling and is more than a minute old, the banner says "Checked at 3:14 PM. Nothing is queued, so it is not checked again until you press Re-check or start work." Lane chips add "(checked 3:14 PM)". An unchanged answer that someone asked for is still pushed, so its time reaches every window.
- **Parks.** A park uses the last preflight read only when it is recent (`recentRead`), so a stale "clear" never makes a job wait for an edge it could miss.

**Waiting its turn.** Each preflight read yields the card's holder (card-holder.ts `holderOf`): another client's job, a lease, a live session, or a card taking no work. ContentStudio's own work is never a holder. The holder goes on the lane chip as `holder`.

- **Time left.** Crucible states no finish time, so it is measured: progress moved between this app's first and latest reads of the same hold, on this app's own clock. The answer is "time left not known yet" until two reads show it moving, and "time left unknown" for a hold with no progress. A new holder id starts a new measurement, so the row follows whoever got the card first.
- **The row.** One row per server that a parked job of this queue waits on sits under the jobs: "Waiting for mac: crucible-cli is running a voice conversion (rvc) — 43% · about 12 min left" (job-activity.ts `holderWaitLine`, `waitingTurnRows`). It updates on each read and is gone once the job takes the card.

**Checks.** New keeper `tools/test-crucible-polling.js` (9 checks, fake Crucible, short intervals): idle → no request after the first; a queue count on/off; a parked job polls until it runs, then stops; admission takes one fresh probe, and none when fresh; a renderer refresh is one probe; holder, time left and swap to a new holder. Three wording checks were added to `check:pure`. Keeper updated: test-crucible-readiness's push check (an unchanged on-demand answer is now pushed). test-crucible-no-server drives `crucible:queue-count`. The fake's busy job takes an id. Built and checked offline, not launched. [readiness.ts; lanes.ts; card-holder.ts; probe.ts; context.ts; crucible-ipc.ts; wire.ts + crucible.types.ts; preload.ts; electron.ts; crucible.ts; job-queue.ts; job-activity.ts; inputs.ts/html/scss; crucible-words.ts; crucible-servers.ts/html; tools/test-crucible-polling.js; tools/test-crucible-readiness.js; tools/test-crucible-no-server.js; tools/fake-crucible.js; tools/routing-publish-checks.js]

**236. A Thumbnails tab for TESTING an automated thumbnail workflow (Owen, 2026-09-28; built on its branch, checked offline, not launched, no Crucible call).** G3's "thumbnails are hand-made and not tested" stands until Owen decides after testing; nothing in the metadata run or publish reads this tab. What was decided and built (docs/thumbnails-lab.md):
- **Frames.** ffmpeg samples ~1/s (≤ 1,800 per run, evenly), 16:9 only (another shape is a crop, Owen's call). Cheap CPU filters first: repeats by a 256-bit difference hash with a 3-level dead zone (flat walls otherwise hash on noise), keeping the sharper copy; blur by Laplacian variance < 35% of the run's median. No CPU face-presence filter: the vision model answers it (Owen). ≤ 120 kept frames are scored, spread in rounds across the range.
- **Scoring (Owen's shape).** ONE frame per `POST /v1/decide` (a multi-image question is ambiguous), the 640x360 sample, five fixed-answer questions sharing the primed state: screen or video, clear face, expression 1-5, eyes open, strong thumbnail; `missing: 'report'`, a frame with a missing letter set aside and named. Ranked by the probabilities, never a threshold, except the screen answer, the ONE filter. Best 20 round-robin across 8 sections, ≥ 4 s apart; every kept frame is also browsable and markable. One lane job (one job per card), one lease, calls at the engine's STATED width (`chat.maxInFlight`, null = 1, cap 8). The request body is packed in one function (`frameDecideBody`) so the coming many-slot decide is a body change only.
- **Words.** New plain-text prompt `shared/pipeline/thumbnails.yml` (`text`, `frames.*`; PROMPT-LEARNINGS Part 13): 2-5 words, capitals, a complete thought that opens a question, adds to the paired title (the operator picks it), from the transcript. Three kinds, one per A/B variant: claim, stakes, reaction, five each; any variant may be picture-only. The report's THUMBNAIL TEXT OPTIONS field is untouched (how to retire it: the doc).
- **Routing.** Two rows in a new `thumbnails` group (`MetadataRoutingTask.group`): `thumbnail_frames` (local vision options only, new `vision` flag; default `qwen3.5-9b-vl`, also 4B/2B/0.8B/27B-vl) and `thumbnail_words` (the thumbnail_text rungs, default the 27B). A metadata run's transcript ceiling, its log line and the dialog's change-all cover the metadata group only. The catalog inventory now carries each model's `modalities`; a text-only reading is "not here" on the frame row. Image refusals (`model_text_only`, `refuse_images_not_served`, `too_many_images`) and a not-installed model surface with model and server named. Before Crucible 1.0.54 no Mac model served image decide; 1.0.54 (live on the Mac 2026-09-28, Owen's choice) adds mlx-vlm logprobs and `qwen3.5-9b-vl` (~0.7 s a question, ~7 min for 100 frames); a text-only refusal names the server's `image_models`. The PC serves 0.8B/2B/4B; the 9B-vl does not usefully fit its 24 GB card.

Face boxes from Apple Vision via Chromium's FaceDetector in a hidden offscreen window (experimental flag on that window only; no helper binary, no Python). Text in the clear rectangle giving the largest letters that avoids every padded face, the reserved reaction slot (bottom right) and logo slot (top right), both configurable and drawn EMPTY; ≤ 2 lines, left-aligned, shrink-to-fit, letters never under 12% of the height: a phrase that cannot keep it is refused in plain words, never shrunk or cut. *(Superseded by #241: the words are always drawn, 1-2 lines, shrunk into the box left of the photo.)* Impact, #FF8000, black outline, soft blurred + darkened patch, optional vignette, all configurable (`thumbnailLab.style`). Saved in `<report folder>/thumbnail tests/`, deliberately not the week's `thumbnails/` (auto-attach never picks a test render). Shown large and at 360/246/168 px.

Trim: alpha ≥ 128 is solid, the largest 8-connected solid group is the person, grown 3 px for the soft edge, everything else cleared (specks dropped and noted). Placed in the reaction space right- and bottom-anchored, 10% allowed off the bottom edge, white outline 10 px at 1080p (configurable, 0 = none); the text then avoids the photo's real bounds. The probabilities are the ranking: every photo listed in order, the top (starred) one pre-selected, none hidden. Photo notes are stored with the folder setting (`thumbnailLab.reactionNotes`), drafts from thumbnails.yml `photo.drafts` until saved. AUTO-COMBINE lays the favourites out as A/B/C, every piece swappable: "Best package" varies everything; "Test one thing" holds two pieces and varies the chosen one (frame, words or photo; needs two favourites of it). **240. Thumbnails move into the metadata run, phase 1: three title and thumbnail pairs made while the models are loaded (Owen, 2026-09-28; built on its branch, checked offline, not launched, no Crucible call).** Owen's rulings: thumbnails are produced during the metadata job; THREE titles and THREE thumbnails go to YouTube Test & Compare's "title and thumbnail" mode, thumbnail n's words complementing title n, never restating it; one look for all three channels; ~4 min of frame scoring per video accepted; THUMBNAIL TEXT OPTIONS retires. G1's "thumbnail text is a pick-field, ten options" and G3's "thumbnails are hand-made and not tested" are superseded by this for the three YouTube channels. What was built (docs/thumbnails-pipeline.md, the contract phase 2 is written against):
- **Stages in the job** (thumbnails/pipeline.ts, on the job's own `JobLeases`): story, frames (CPU) and scoring (the `thumbnail_frames` vision model) for every item before any chapter; after the fields, scrub and gate, words per title (`thumbnail_words`), tone and photo ranking (`thumbnail_judge`) and three renders. One swap (the vision model once, then the text model); a 27B the fields hold is reused for the words and the tone/photo, no reload (the re-roll gate's 9B, when on, sits between: stated). The channel lessons now resolve just before the first chapter (after the scoring) instead of at the top of the run, so a local distillation cannot put a text load before the vision load.
- **The story link for thumbnails** (story-match.ts): manual (the Inputs page's link, never replaced) | name (one story by the finder's title tiers, checked against the transcript: a disagreement links nothing and names both) | transcript (24 spread 12-word windows, normalised 5-gram hits, against the week's exported story transcripts; a clear winner only: ≥ 4 hits, ≥ a quarter of the windows, ≥ 2x the runner-up). Else "no story" with the counts. Recorded on the report only (`thumbnails.story`, `via: 'transcript-match'` added); it is never the content link (#142, #144 stand) and the publish record is not written. NOTE: the tab's rule was "never linked by name"; the brief asked name first, so name is first but checked; Owen may prefer transcript-only.
- **Pairs**: the gate's title ranking when it ran, else the titles as written, top three (recorded); per pair a words call carrying its title; defaults claim / stakes / reaction for pairs 1-3; frames from different scenes, best first; photos drawn from each pair's top 3 with one stored seed; the saved look (`thumbnailLab.style`), the app's logo and photo library.
- **Storage**: the `thumbnails` record (version 1, pipeline-record.ts `readItemThumbnails`) on the item in its job file; files in `<report folder>/thumbnails/<jobId>-<n>/` (scored frames, full frames, three renders), never the week's `thumbnails/` folder, so nothing is proposed or attached before Owen picks (#219). `picks: []` (ordered, ≤ 3) for phase 2; `OutputHandlerService.updateItemThumbnails` is the one write door.
- **States and failures**: made | no-story (reason; not a failure) | off (per-run `thumbnails: false`, a channel `thumbnails: false`, a channel file without the key, a caller without the setup, a compilation, unreadable tab settings; each said) | failed (the stage and its reason in plain words, and the run's warnings; the item is saved, as the chapters and the scrub do, #148, #223). A stop is rethrown, never recorded.
- **THUMBNAIL TEXT OPTIONS**: `thumbnail_text` removed from the `fields:`/`counts:` of telltale, unfiltered and fireside; every channel file now says `thumbnails: true|false`. Its field definition, routing row, re-roll button and readers stay for older reports until phase 2 removes the UI. The routing dialog heading no longer says the thumbnail rows are unused by metadata runs.
- **Moved, not duplicated**: caption lines (prompts.ts), frame ids and scene labels (frame-sampler.ts), file names (renderer.ts) out of lab-service; the scorer's worker is `scoreFramesOnCard` under a caller's job (the tab's lane job and the metadata job both call it).
- **Checks**: new tools/thumbnail-pipeline-checks.js (11: transcript link winner / ambiguous / too little / name agreeing and disagreeing / manual kept; stage order with one swap and no 27B reload over the fake Crucible inside one lane job; words per title and the gate's order; no story; a failed scoring stage surfaced and a stop rethrown; off states and the shipped channel flags; storage round-trip and refused picks), in check:thumbnail-lab; the render smoke checks the Electron renderer adapter (15); routing-publish-checks updated for the retired field. Open: phase 2's IPC (the doc lists it), the per-run switch in the queue's UI, imported story transcripts get no story, an item delete does not remove its thumbnails folder yet, no live timing. **241. Thumbnails phase 2: the reports page's Thumbnails window, ordered picks, publishing pick 1, text that always fits, and the test tab retired (Owen, 2026-09-28; built on its branch 2026-09-28/29, checked offline, not launched, no Crucible call).** Owen's rulings: a Thumbnails pop-up from the metadata report; ordered picks 1/2/3 like the titles (pair n = title n + thumbnail n; pick 1 the primary with no A/B test); swaps redrawn at once; "Rewrite words for this title" (27B on demand) when the title order changes; text always drawn, 1 or 2 lines, shrunk, in the box from the left margin to the reaction photo and up to the top, off faces where possible, never refused ("text will never be way too long … if it does I'll change the prompt"); manual upload stays and can be a pick; no story: 1-3 of his screenshots make that many thumbnails; deleting a report removes its thumbnails folder; one look for all three channels; pick 1 through the existing publish thumbnail path, picks 2-3 beside it for Test & Compare in Studio; the tab and the old THUMBNAIL TEXT OPTIONS UI removed. What was built (docs/thumbnails-pipeline.md, "Phase 2"; docs/thumbnails-lab.md marked retired):
- **The window** (MatDialog, frontend components/thumbnails-window/; opened from a new Thumbnails block under the titles, which shows the record's line, the picks and any pick whose words were written for another title). Pairs as pictures (click to pick/unpick, click order = pick order, the titles' rule); a pick strip pairing pick n with chosen title n live, "Rewrite words for this title" on a mismatch; "Use my own image…" (validated by the thumbnail door, read in place); per pair: the scene strip (2 per scene, More), words per kind or typed or No text, photos ranked with % (and for which words), No photo, a new top-3 draw, the logo switch, "Write the words again for another title"; screenshots for a no-story or failed record. Closing it gives the held 27B back (also on quit, after 5 idle minutes, or when another model is needed).
- **Main process**: report-thumbnails.ts (one action per item at a time; every write through `updateItemThumbnails`; swaps draw a NEW file beside the old, a picked pair's pick follows; `picks/Pick 1..n` copies rewritten on every save; words and tone/photo on ONE held lease; screenshots through pipeline.ts `ItemThumbnailRun.fromScreenshots`, cut to 16:9 around the centre and said), thumbnails-ipc.ts (`thumbnails:*`), pipeline.ts `drawPair` shared by the run and the window. Record version 1 extended (no record had picks): nullable phrase/kind/photo/draw, `rankedFor`, `source.video: null` for screenshots, picks `{ kind: 'made', pair, file, wordsFor } | { kind: 'own', file }` (no file or pair twice, at most 3).
- **Publishing**: pick 1's copy is set through `PublishState.setThumbnail` (manual source; the existing upload path and #219 limits) whenever pick 1 changes; with no picks, a thumbnail that was pick 1's copy is cleared and Owen's own choice left. Picks 2-3 are listed on the Thumbnail row "For your A/B test" with Show the picks (no API for Test & Compare).
- **Text rules** (layout.ts): the text box (left margin to the photo's drawn left edge, top to bottom margin), MAX_LINES 3 → 2, face-free placement when it holds the words at 7%, else the whole box at ≤ 7% and smaller until it fits, top or bottom by least face covered, said in the render's notes. `renderThumbnail` never refuses. Supersedes the Thumbnails tab entry's (#236) "too long is refused" and the same day's three lines.
- **The look** moved out of lab-service.ts into look.ts (store keys unchanged, so Owen's saved look and notes stay; the library stays in `<userData>/thumbnail-lab/`); the Thumbnail look dialog opens from the window, the Thumbnails block and Settings › Thumbnails.
- **Delete**: `deleteItem` removes the folder the record names when it sits directly in `<report folder>/thumbnails/` (and that folder once empty), else leaves it and says why (receipt `thumbnailsFolderRemoved` / `thumbnailsReason`); `deleteJobTxtFiles` (history delete, four-week prune) does the same per item.
- **Removed**: the tab (route, sidebar entry, component), lab-service.ts, thumbnail-lab-ipc.ts, combine.ts, every `thumbs:*` channel; the reports page's THUMBNAIL TEXT OPTIONS section and its export line (older reports show nothing for it; the field definition and readers stay).

**242. The Thumbnails window rebuilt as one top-to-bottom flow; stopped runs finish from what they store; every failure on screen (Owen, 2026-09-29; built on its branch the same day, checked offline, looked at on scratch data with the fake Crucible, no live Crucible call).** Owen tried #241's window: "im clicking things and nothing is doing anything at all ... the images it gathered from the original section should be at the top. i pick three. the text it generated. i pick three. it overlays them." His run had stopped at `tone-photos` (empty photo library; the copy-into-app offer lived in the removed tab), the window offered the pair editor on that stopped record, and every refusal ("There are no thumbnails to change: ...") reached only the log. What was built (docs/thumbnails-pipeline.md, "The window rebuilt as one flow"):
- **The flow**: one line saying what to do; 1 Frames (two per scene, More, up to three in click order, badges, the rest close up); 2 Text (every generated line in one list labelled with its kind and title, No text, typed words; up to three in click order); 3 Photos (optional, per thumbnail, ranked with %, else the top-3 draw, No photo, Logo); 4 Your thumbnails (thumbnail n = frame n + text n + photo n + logo, drawn on the CPU as soon as picked, large and phone-size). **Saved as you go** (not a Save button): the drawn thumbnails are the ordered picks, pick 1 set through `PublishState.setThumbnail` when its source changes. Own image per place, Rewrite words for this title, screenshots, Start from the suggested three. Picking rules pure in `thumbnails-compose.ts`.
- **Failures on screen**: one `ActionRunner` for every call (one at a time; spinner and running clock; a failure is a sticky banner "<what> failed: <sentence>"); disabled buttons carry their reason in text. Too few reaction photos: a banner with Thumbnail look.
- **Finish making thumbnails** (`thumbnails:finish`): `resumePlan` keeps each stage whose output is stored (frames and scoring kept or run together: the scene rows need unstored colour signatures), runs the rest via `ItemThumbnailRun.resume`, on ONE held job given back after; refused before any model call with fewer than two photos or no Crucible server (`lanes.gpuVenue`), the reason in the view. **Make thumbnails again from scratch** (`thumbnails:remake`), own picks kept.
- **Pipeline**: the empty library stays a stop (no fallback), now checked before the tone call and naming where to add photos (`photosMissingReason`); rewrite and screenshots check it first. `PairChange.wordsFor` / `default.wordsFor` (words written for any title; the pick's `wordsFor` follows) and `rankingOf` (draw from the words' pair's ranking). A redrawn pair's old render is removed once no pick points at it (supersedes #241's "the old file stays").
- **Bug found by the keeper**: a thumbnail's "No photo" (null) read back as the draw (`?? 'auto'`); now only an absent choice is the draw.
- **Checks**: thumbnail-pipeline-checks.js +4 (errors reach the window; Finish resumes only missing stages; from scratch; picking order to overlays) and the swap check updated; build:all, check:thumbnail-lab (44 + 22 + 15), check:thumbnail, check:pure, check:crucible, check:p4, routing-publish-checks pass. Screenshots on scratch data: stopped banner with the empty-library reason, Finish running and finished on the fake Crucible, three hand-picked overlays, picks read back on reopening, a draw failure as a banner. Open: frames/texts picked beyond the drawn places are not stored; a words swap still uses the words' pair's ranking, not a new one. No frame was ever scored for real. Supersedes #240's `scoring` stage and one-swap ordering, #241-#243's ranked frame rows, best-first grid, "Show more" and ranking-chosen default frames, and #243's "the defaults are still drawn" for a story. What was built (docs/thumbnails-pipeline.md, "The frame scoring removed"):
- **Removed**: the `scoring` stage (stages: story, frames, words, render; `scoring` joins `tone-photos` in `RETIRED_STAGES`), frame-scorer.ts, frame-ranking.ts, thumbnails.yml `frames.*` and prompts.ts's frame-question code, frame-scenes.ts `allocateScoring` / `framesToScore` / `sceneRows`, `MAX_FRAMES_TO_SCORE`, pipeline.ts `defaultFrames`, the setup's Crucible doors, the record's `scoring` / `bestScenes` and per-frame `score` / `reading` / `flag`, the window's scores, "Show more" and `thumbnails:frames` channel. Routing: the `thumbnail_frames` row and the four vision rungs offered only on it (`qwen35-9b-vl`, `qwen35-2b`, `qwen35-08b`, `qwen38-27b-vl`), `visionAvailability` and the `vision` flag; a stored selection is dropped loudly with a write-back (REMOVED_ROUTING_TASKS / REMOVED_ROUTING_OPTIONS). The transport's generic `decideItems` and image support stay (unused now).
- **The grid, on the CPU** (frame-scenes.ts `gridFrames` / `gridQuota`): after the unchanged sampling, blur and repeat filters and scene grouping, each scene offers two frames (`GRID_PER_SCENE`), one when on screen under `SHORT_SCENE_SECONDS` (the short-scene rule kept from the allocation), never more than it kept; chosen by `thinAcrossRange` over the scene's time on screen: the sharpest (Laplacian variance) of each half, so a revisited scene shows both visits. Only those frames stay on disk; the record's `frames` is the grid in time order (`scenes[].shown`), shown so.
- **No default frame, no fallback**: a story's pairs get `frameId` / `scene` null ("No frame picked yet: pick one in the Thumbnails window."), the render stage draws only pairs with a frame (screenshot pairs), and the line says to pick the frames and photos. Generate stays gated on a picked frame and text; drawing or rewriting a pair with no frame is refused by name; a saved pick on a frameless pair is refused on reading. "Start from the suggested three" became "Start from the suggested words" (texts only). Nothing chooses a frame in Owen's place.
- **Older records**: fields read and ignored; a record stopped at `scoring` keeps its story and frames (its scored frames are its grid) and Finish runs words and render, the view saying the step is gone; resuming a story record clears the frames the ranking gave its pairs.
