**247. The Thumbnails window becomes a card editor: three live cards filled by clicking, each piece zoomed or moved, saved with one button (Owen, 2026-09-29: "maybe i select the thumbnail card i want to fill. then i select the frame, the text, and the image of myself to use in it. then i click a different card and do the same ... i should be able to hit a zoom button on a frame and resize (zoom/shrink) or reposition any of the three elements. logo goes in top right automatically, border goes on top of the image automatically and neither of those two should be edited. as soon as i click something, it adds it to the frame. if i unclick it, it removes it from the frame"; built on its branch the same day, checked offline, not launched, no model call, not merged).** Supersedes #243-#246's pick lists 1/2/3, Generate thumbnails, "Start from the suggested words", "Clear picks", No text / No photo and the logo switch, and #246's kept `thumbnails:remake`. What was built (docs/thumbnails-pipeline.md, "The card editor"):
- **The window**: three cards across the top (card n = pair n), each a live 1280x720 preview with the title its pick number goes with (chosen titles first) and its state; one active (card 1 on opening). Trays below (Frames, Text with typed words, Photos): a click puts the item on the active card at once, a click on what it has takes it off; badges name the cards using each item; one item may sit on several cards. ⤢ Edit opens a larger editor: drag to move, wheel / Size slider / corner handle to resize the frame (zoom in = crop, out = smaller on black), the text box (words refitted, 1-2 lines, as large as the box holds) and the photo; Reset per piece; logo and border never editable. **Save thumbnails** draws every card with a frame (per-card spinner via `progress.card`) and saves the cards in order as the picks (an empty or frameless card left out, said on the card, the rest closing up; Pick 1..n files and publishing pick 1 unchanged); a card that cannot be drawn saves nothing and is named. Per card: Clear, Undo changes, Use my own image… (checked at once), Rewrite words for this title (words only, nothing drawn). Closing with unsaved changes asks in the window (Save and close / Close without saving / Keep editing; `disableClose`, no browser dialog). Kept: prepare on opening, Thumbnail look…, screenshots (they become the Frames tray).
- **One layout, one drawing**: layout.ts moved to `electron/shared/thumbnail-layout.ts` and gained `composeThumbnail` (the renderer's old arithmetic when there is no edit, checked step for step) and `planTextIn`; canvas-page.ts's page functions moved to `electron/shared/thumbnail-draw.ts` (`paintThumbnail`, `measurePhrase`), run by source in the hidden page and called directly by the window's preview through `thumbnail-shared.ts` (the master-timeline-map pattern). The preview's pieces come from the main process: the full frame (extracted atomically) with Apple Vision's faces on that same file, searched once per frame (a failed search said on the card), photos trimmed as rendered, the logo at its drawn size, the border. Remaining difference by construction: a 1280-wide JPEG frame and ≤ 900 px photos in the window (sharpness only).
- **Stored**: `pairs[].default.adjust?` = `{ frame?: { x, y, scale }, text?: { x, y, w, h }, photo?: { cx, cy, h } }` in fractions of the picture, validated by name on read and on save (never clamped); absent in old records, which render exactly as before. Own-image picks gain `card`. `renderThumbnail` / `ThumbnailRenderer` / `drawPair` take `adjust`; the run's render stage passes a screenshot pair's.
- **IPC**: + `frame-detail`, `photo-detail`, `save-cards`, `closed`; `choose-own` answers `{ file, picture }`; `pair-title` takes the kind and answers `{ view, text }`; − `render-pair`, `save-picks`, `remake`, `release-model`, with `renderPair`, `savePicks`, `remake`, `start`'s from-scratch folder, `PairChange`, `PickRequest` and the window's slot/pick helpers.
- **Checks**: thumbnail-pipeline-checks.js 25 (save in card order and the gap, a failed card saving nothing, refusals, edits stored and honoured, the preview's pieces, own image by card, rewrite drawing nothing, screenshots, the card rules, read-back equality and unsaved changes, old picks, the window's shape incl. no teal, storage refusals); thumbnail-lab-checks.js 33 (+1 composeThumbnail); render smoke 18 (+2: frame zoom/pan/shrink, text box and moved photo change the pixels where expected, an empty edit byte-identical to none; the preview's paint equals the page's). build:electron, build:preload, frontend build, check:thumbnail-lab, check:pure, check:thumbnail, check:retired pass. Unverified: nobody has seen the window; the feel of dragging and resizing, and the saved PNG against the card, need a human look. **248. Model routing names a model, not a quant: the job's Crucible server's catalog picks the one build it holds (Owen, 2026-09-29: "crucible's job is to select the model quant that works for the system we're using. wsl uses 4 bit, mac uses 8 bit. it doenst need to tell the user that"; "theres only one quant available on mac, one available on pc. we can retrieve the quant by listing the available model on each crucible server. content studio shouldnt request a model that isnt available on a crucible server"; built on branch `routing-model-not-quant` the same day, checked offline with the fake Crucible, not launched, no live Crucible or model call, not merged).** The dialog had said "qwen3.8-27b-8bit.toml has no cuda-linux block ... Thumbnail words is refused by name when it runs" because the routing named the 8-bit 27B while its "Runs on" server was WSL. What was built (docs/crucible/routing-server.md, "Models, not quants"):
- **One option per model**: table rows are `MetadataRoutingOptionDef` {kind, label, `crucibleIds` (a fixed, reviewed candidate list), `cliModel`}; no row carries an id a caller can send. `qwen38-27b` "Qwen 3.8 · 27B" stands for `qwen3.8-27b-8bit` and `qwen3.8-27b-4bit`; the 9B/4B and Claude rows have their one id; labels name no bit width. The 8-bit option `qwen38-27b-8bit` is gone; every 27B row (titles, description, chapters, thumbnail_text, pinned_comment, thumbnail_words) defaults to `qwen38-27b`. `SUMMARIZATION_MODEL` (a quant id) became `SUMMARIZATION_OPTION = 'qwen38-27b'`.
- **Resolution, one function** (`resolveLocalOption`): the candidates against one server's catalog; exactly one installed runs; none is "not on <server>" (`pullable` when one could be downloaded, else `not-here`), refused by name; more than one is `ambiguous`, refused by name, nothing picked (Owen decides if a server ever holds two); an unreadable catalog is `unknown`. Used by the dialog (`optionAvailability`/`buildRoutingView`: a resolved option shows installed with the build as its `model`, no quant sentence; new `ambiguous` flag; row note "<note> <row> won't run until you pick a model this server offers") and by every run.
- **Every run binds through `RoutingModels`**, read ONCE at the start of the job from the job's server (`routing-models.ts` `readRoutingModels`: `lanes.gpuVenue()`, the catalog via the new `lanes.inventoryFor`), cached for the job; `bind(task, option)` returns the bound option with the resolved id, else throws `RoutedModelUnavailableError` (`routed_model_not_here|ambiguous|unknown`) before anything is loaded or sent. Each local option is logged once per job ("Qwen 3.8 · 27B → qwen3.8-27b-4bit on "wsl""). A job routing no local model reads no catalog. `routingOption`, `resolveOperatorOption`, `resolveChapterModelOption`, `resolveCompilationPackagingOption` and `resolveSnapChapterModels` require the resolution, so the compiler found every site: the metadata generator (field units, description, chapters both engines, scrub, re-roll gate, guidelines distiller, compilation package, and the compilation summarizer, now resolved inside the job instead of in ipc-handlers), the thumbnail words (`ThumbnailJobDoors.models`), the reports page's field re-roll + cleanup, more titles, soften, cleanup and Thumbnails window, and the editor's stories, story titles and routed-model line. The in-queue split writes no titles and binds nothing (`resolveSnapBoundaryModels`). The fixed 9B scorers are unchanged.
- **Migration**: `MERGED_ROUTING_OPTIONS` rewrites a stored `qwen38-27b-8bit` to `qwen38-27b` with one logged line and a write-back (said once); a stored `qwen38-27b` (the old 4-bit option's id) keeps its row and now resolves per server; the modal's strict save refuses the old id.

The extension's A/B fill puts the saved thumbnails into Studio's "Title and thumbnail" test beside the chosen titles (Owen, 2026-09-30, deferred from #241 to a session with a live Studio window; Studio's dialog verified live that day by the coordinating session; built on branch `ab-thumbnails` the same day, checked offline, the extension not loaded, Studio not opened, the app not launched, not merged).** Pair n = chosen title n + Pick n (read live, #241). Owen's ruling mid-build: "Owen may save only ONE thumbnail" — one saved pick is a titles-only test, not a refusal. What was built (docs/thumbnails-pipeline.md, "The saved picks, for the A/B fill"):
- **App**: report-thumbnails.ts `locateItem` (the window's lookup, now shared) and `abTestPickFiles`; publish-bridge `getAbThumbnails` (injected reader; each pick through `fitThumbnailFile` like `getThumbnail`; none is `[]`; a saved pick missing, unusable or out of order throws by name, never a shorter list) and `ItemDetail.abThumbnails` (the count; a damaged record fails the detail, never 0); `GET /publish/ab-thumbnails?itemId` → `{ picks }` on the ingest server.
- **Which test** (extension ab-plan.ts `planAbTest`, pure): 0 or 1 pick → "Title only" (chip-0; one pick is the video's own thumbnail, set by the Thumbnail action, and the result says so); 2-3 picks equal to the titles → "Title and thumbnail" (chip-2); 2-3 that differ → refused before anything is touched, in plain words; an app without the count → "update the app", never 0. The images are fetched and recounted before the dialog opens.
- **In Studio** (fillers.ts, action renamed "A/B test", its button saying which test it will set): the chip clicked by position and confirmed `aria-checked`, every time (the dialog opened on "Title and thumbnail" by default); each row found structurally (nearest ancestor of the title slot with exactly one `ytcp-thumbnail-uploader`), strictly inside the dialog; each Pick set through thumbnail.ts `setThumbnailOnInput` (generalised from `setStudioThumbnail`) and confirmed by the input holding it AND the row's picture changing to a new `data:image/` preview; Set test must enable (waited 10 s; its absence is a failure now, where the old code passed silently). The main thumbnail filler skips any file input inside a dialog holding more than one uploader, so it can never write into the A/B rows.
- **"Run a new test?"** (a test already exists; Continue deletes it on the server at once, verified live): recognised by shape (a new dialog with no title slots and no chip bar, held ~1 s), never pressed; the shelf says what Studio asks (a new waiting line, `say`/`note`) and waits 2 minutes for Owen; closed with no A/B dialog within 8 s ends the fill, said. Set test, Continue and Save stay the operator's.
- **Found, then fixed the same day (coordinating session)**: saving picks sets the publish record's thumbnailPath to `picks/Pick 1.png` only from the Thumbnails window's renderer (`followPublish`), when pick 1's source changed and the Publish panel's current item is the same item; otherwise the main Thumbnail action serves whatever thumbnailPath already was. Fixed: `followPublish` writes Pick 1 to the report through `publish-set-thumbnail` whichever report the Publish panel shows (through the panel when it shows this one, so it updates), and a refusal is thrown so the next save tries again. A report whose Pick 1 was skipped before is set on its next save that changes Pick 1.
- **Checks**: new tools/ab-thumbnails-checks.js (`npm run check:ab-thumbnails`, 15: the bridge in order/empty/missing/unusable/out of order/no item, the count on the detail, the real route over the ingest server, ab-plan bundled with the extension's esbuild); thumbnail-pipeline-checks.js reads `abTestPickFiles` beside the save (still 27). build:electron, build:preload, extension typecheck and build, check:pure, check:thumbnail, check:thumbnail-lab, check:retired, check:crucible (24; test-crucible-acts needs CRUCIBLE_CAPABILITY_PY from a worktree) pass. **252. Reaction photos: each trim is kept on disk beside its photo, so opening the Thumbnails window no longer freezes the app after a start (coordinating session, 2026-09-30, Owen: "find out whats causing the hanging that content studio is experiencing. its happening again, after a restart").** Measured on Owen's 13 photos: trimming runs on the main process (nativeImage decode plus a per-pixel pass in JavaScript), 0.4-0.9 s a photo, about 7.5 s in all, and the trims were kept only in memory, so the first `look.photos()` after every start (the Thumbnails window's photo tray) blocked the whole app, the extension's requests included; each later call still re-made the 13 small pictures (about 0.3 s). Now `reaction-photos.ts` keeps each trim in `<photos>/.trimmed/<file>.<mtime>-<size>.{png,json}` (the trimmed PNG, its size, the note and the picker's small picture): the second start read all 13 in about 0 ms. A changed photo has a new key and is trimmed again (its older kept files removed); a removed photo's kept files go with it (photo-library.ts `removePhoto`); a kept file that cannot be read is trimmed again and said in the log; a trim that cannot be kept is used and the log says why. Stray ums are judged on the pieces as they play after Owen's cuts (coordinating session, 2026-09-30, Owen: "did we add logic to remove 'uh' or 'um' if theyre standalone cuts? theres one that made it into the seth gruber story ... after my edits").** #238 looked only at auto-editor's pieces, so an um that Owen's own editor cuts isolated INSIDE one piece ("Period. Yeah," with the words either side cut) was never offered. `findStrayFillers` now takes his cuts (original seconds) and first splits each piece around them (`playingPieces`: a cut inside a piece makes two pieces that no longer connect; a wholly cut piece is gone), then applies #238's rule unchanged. One addition: a word reaching less than `MIN_TOUCH_SECONDS` (50 ms) into a piece is a sliver a hand-placed cut left of its neighbour, not speech the piece carries. The um Owen pointed at was not one this could have found: his isolating cuts were made in Final Cut after export (the editor's saved edits held one cut), and the session's transcript (Qwen3-ASR) has no um there at all ("more than just a pregnancy"). check:strays 19/19 (+4). When it is on and the session is transcribed, `onExport` first runs the top bar's `cutStrayFillers` (the #238/#253 rule, unchanged: a lone um/uh that is the only word in a piece with a cut on each side) as ONE undo step in the editor, then exports `this.cuts` with them in, so the timeline and the export agree and ⌘Z brings them back. **255. Crucible 1.0.76: every lease is now ONE queue session per job per server; parking is gone, the job waits in the server's own line, and a session the server ends fails the stage by name (coordinating session, 2026-10-01, urgent: 1.0.76 is deployed on the Mac and the PC and leases are gone from both, so every job that took a lease failed; built on branch `crucible-1076-sessions` off `crucible` the same day, checked offline with the fake Crucible, no live server, the app not launched, not merged).** SDK 1.0.55 → 1.0.76 (pre-release, named explicitly) through `tools/adopt-crucible-release.mjs 1.0.76`; the module JSON regenerated by crucible's own `gen-modules.py` at the v1.0.76 tag (content hash unchanged, `1.0.76+7de7dfd8ce35`). What was built (MIGRATION.md and docs/QUEUE.md as Crucible wrote them; the design is `electron/crucible/session.ts`'s header):
- **The session** (`session.ts`, replacing `lease.ts`): `ServerSessions` holds at most ONE open session per server for this install. A lane job owns its session (lanes.ts `sessionOn`: asked for by the job's first GPU work, ASR included, kept for the whole run, closed in `runJob`'s finally); anything else that needs the server while it is open (the editor's story title, the reports page's more titles, the Thumbnails window's words, the editor's voice isolation, a transcription outside a job) JOINS it, and a standalone action with nothing open holds one only while it works. Crucible's two requirements, as the coordinator relayed them: the client name is unique and stable per install, `contentstudio@<host>` (client-factory.ts `clientNameFor`: lower-case, macOS `.local` dropped; `isOurClient` is exact, so another install's ContentStudio is another app), because membership is matched on it; and a second session from our client while the first is open would queue BEHIND it and is never asked for (sessions never merge; denoise, ASR and standalone calls reuse the open one). `JobSessions` (was `JobLeases`; `JobModelLifecycle.sessions`, `ThumbnailJobDoors.sessions`, `withJobSession`) pins the job's server and makes each model resident INSIDE the session (`CardSession.ensure`: the #209/#220 grow/shrink rules and the capability question before a load, unchanged; models inside one session load and evict as the job needs). Every local item carries `X-Crucible-Session` (the SDK's `CrucibleSession`; the raw chat door through `engineFetch({session})`), so an ended session answers `409 session_closed` instead of running as a plain request.
- **No fallbacks**: a session the server ends (`idle`, `operator`, `max_hold`, `server_restart`; or one that never opened, `expired`/`load_failed`) fails the job's next call `session_closed` naming the reason; nothing reopens a session and carries on (`lease_lost` is gone with the heartbeat, the TTL and the 2 s release grace). A server that does not list `queue.sessions` is refused `needs_update` by name (feature detection, `client.has`). chat-stream.ts reads `session_closed` / `session_open` / `server_busy` door `session` into the SDK's own `CrucibleSessionClosed` / `CrucibleSessionHeld`.
- **The idle rule (one)**: every session says `idle_s` 900, and while a lane job runs a cloud call (`claude -p`, an `anthropic/` field through a Crucible) the lanes touch its open session every 30 s (`SESSION_TOUCH_EVERY_MS`), because work on this side is not activity on the server's. Any other gap on this side (ffmpeg, the editor's Python between denoise chunks) has the 900 s. A lane job may wait a day in the line (`max_wait_s` 86400); a standalone call the server's default hour.
- **Parking removed** (`parking.ts` deleted): another app on the server is no longer a park. `session()` waits in the server's line, `onQueue` puts the place on the lane chip (`LaneChip.inLine {jobId, position, of}`: "In line, 2 of 3") and on the job's row (a `generation-progress` line of phase `waiting`, "Waiting in line on mac: 2 of 3 (another app's work is ahead)", then "Crucible on mac is free; starting"), and the waiting-its-turn row (#234) shows under a job in line too, with the holder (now `job` or `session`; `lease`, `claim` and `card` kinds are gone) and its place. A Stop takes the job out of the line. Only a venue still parks (nothing selected or pinned, paused, not answering), re-decided at each plan. `accelerator_busy` / measured `insufficient_memory`, which used to park, now fail by name if they reach a job inside its session.
- **Events, not polling**: the lanes' 15 s `/v1/activity` preflight became `server-watch.ts`, following each running server's `GET /v1/events` (snapshot, then `job.*`/`session.*`/`card.*` folded into a `CardView`, card-holder.ts) only while work is queued (#234's rule, unchanged), gated on `client.has('events')`; a server without it is said on its chip by name and never polled in its place. The renderer's ask (`readAll`) is still one `/v1/activity` read. Readiness's own probe timer is unchanged (it is reachability, not the strip); the probe's one activity read names another app's open session as busy.
- **Ledger and sweeps**: rows are `job` or `session` (a session row is the server's, `jobId` empty); the sweep cancels jobs then ends sessions (`removeFromQueue`), and no longer reads activity to ask for an `unload-model` (closing a session settles the card on the server). A `lease` row an older build left is dropped on reading, said in the log. Quit aborts the jobs, waits ~2 s, closes every session still open (`closeAll`: a held title loop, the editor's isolation), then sweeps. CLIs end their sessions on SIGINT/SIGTERM through their ledger.
- **Denoise** runs outside any queue job (the editor's workflow): the pass asks for (or joins) a session once its first chunk is uploaded, keeps the separator resident in it across the track, and lets go on `dispose`; the lease/heartbeat seam, `parkOnProbe` and the `parked` progress kind are gone (`in_line` in their place). ASR uploads first, then holds the session from the submit to the job's end.
- **Not done (optional)**: one `decideItems` for the Thumbnails frame checks: no such call site exists any more (#241's frame scoring stage was removed), so nothing to fold.
- **Checks**: check:crucible 23 keepers, 237 checks (test-crucible-parking.js removed; lanes, transport, in-flight, interrupt, polling, shrink, denoise, probe, engine-resolve, stall-clock and vendor-pin rewritten for sessions); the fake Crucible speaks 1.0.76's sessions, `/v1/events`, `features` and the session gate, and settles the card when a session closes. check:pure, check:asr (+1: the upload goes before the session; `session_closed` names its reason), check:p4, check:digest, check:chaptering, check:reroll, check:retired, check:legacy-gone, check:mutes, check:strays, thumbnail-pipeline-checks (27), thumbnail-lab-checks (33), thumbnail-checks (25) pass; build:electron, build:preload and the frontend build clean. Not run: the Electron smoke scripts (check:thumbnail's fit smoke, check:thumbnail-lab's render smoke) and check:ab-thumbnails (this worktree has no extension node_modules). Unverified: everything against the real 1.0.76 servers (no live test without Owen's OK); whether a real server refuses an UPSTREAM chat `session_open` while another app's session is open (the fake does, and ContentStudio then fails the cloud call `busy` naming the holder rather than wait in the line). [crucible/session.ts (new), server-watch.ts (new), lease.ts and parking.ts (deleted), lanes.ts, transport.ts, card-holder.ts, client-factory.ts, chat-stream.ts, errors.ts, in-flight-ledger.ts, in-flight-sweep.ts, context.ts, asr.ts, asr-venue.ts, denoise.ts, probe.ts, readiness.ts, wire.ts, cli-lanes.ts, crucible-ipc.ts, venue-decision.ts, acts.ts, context-check.ts, transport-failure.ts; main.ts; ipc/ipc-handlers.ts; services/metadata (model-lifecycle, ai-manager, metadata-generator, metadata-tasks, description-unit, chapter-whole-transcript, snap-chapters, reroll, rewrite-pass, context-sizing, metadata-routing), services/thumbnails (pipeline, report-thumbnails, words-writer), services/editor (story-ipc, editor-ipc, separation-protocol), services/transcription/crucible-transcription.ts; frontend inputs.ts/.html, job-activity.ts, job-queue.ts, crucible.types.ts (mirror), editor.component.ts; package.json, vendor/ tarballs, shared/crucible module JSON + toml; tools/fake-crucible.js, _crucible-keeper.js, the keepers named above, asr-checks.js, routing-publish-checks.js, thumbnail-pipeline-checks.js, and the live tools (chaptering-run, crucible-raw-client, crucible-raw-denoise-client, crucible-denoise-live, crucible-p3-live, reroll-calibrate, snap-live, asr-acceptance, generate-metadata-cli); CRUCIBLE-MIGRATION-PLAN.md §13, docs/crucible/README.md]

**256. Crucible SDK 1.0.76 → 1.0.80 (Latest), and the restart sweep closes a recorded session with `closeSession` (coordinating session, 2026-10-02, Owen: "make sure everything is up to date and configured to use the latest crucible changes").**
- **Repin.** `node tools/adopt-crucible-release.mjs 1.0.80`. `shared/crucible/contentstudio.module.json` was regenerated by the v1.0.80 tag's own `scripts/gen-modules.py`, run from a `git archive v1.0.80` extract in a scratch venv. Only its version moves: `1.0.80+7de7dfd8ce35`, content hash unchanged. The toml's provenance note now names v1.0.80.
- **What 1.0.77–1.0.80 changed, as it touches ContentStudio:**
  - Every request that can wait now waits by default. A request opts out with `queue: false`, and ContentStudio sends no `queue` member anywhere, so nothing changes for it.
  - A chat for an upstream (cloud) model never waits and is never refused `session_open`. That settles #255's open question in favour of the cloud call; the fake Crucible still refuses one, and no keeper depends on that.
  - New: `closeSession(id)`, `fleetSession()`, decide batched on mlx-lm, and job types switched off in config refused at the door.
- **`closeSession(id)`.** `in-flight-sweep.ts` now closes a session row through `DELETE /v1/queue/sessions/{id}`, which the server records as reason `client`, this app closing its own session. `removeFromQueue` recorded it as `operator`. Closing a session that is already closed answers its state rather than refusing. The in-flight keeper asserts `client`.
- **`fleetSession()` is not used.** ContentStudio's venue for a job is the routing's server, never whichever opens first (#205, #222).

**257. The thumbnail drawing page is a plain hidden window, no longer an offscreen one (Owen, 2026-10-04: "crash").**
- **The crash.** ContentStudio died with `ContentStudio-2026-10-04-112143.ips`: EXC_BAD_ACCESS (KERN_INVALID_ADDRESS at 0x1) in `objc_release` on CrBrowserMain, under V8 frames. That is the same signature as the earlier crash after a thumbnail was made. It came 21 s after the log's last line, "[Thumbnails] … saved the cards (1 made, 2 made, 3 made)".
- **Why the page is suspected.** Every save draws through `ThumbnailCanvas` (canvas-page.ts), a hidden `BrowserWindow` with `webPreferences.offscreen: true`, and destroys it in the save's `finally` (`renderer.close()`). An objc object released from the main thread after JS ran, seconds after an offscreen window was destroyed, points at the offscreen window's teardown when its wrapper is collected. That is suspected, not proven: the scratch repro hit it once in many runs.
- **The fix.** The page never needed offscreen rendering. It draws on its own canvas and hands back `toDataURL`, and faces come from the Shape Detection API, so it is now `show: false` with no `offscreen`. backgroundThrottling stays off.
- **Checks.** check:thumbnail (10) and check:thumbnail-lab's Electron half (18, including Apple Vision on the reference frame and the evidence render) pass on the plain hidden page. thumbnail-pipeline-checks pass (27). build:electron is clean.
- **Open.** If it crashes again after a save, the next step is to keep one page for the app's life rather than destroy one per save.

**258. YouTube's description limit is checked before an upload or push, and named with what to cut (Owen, 2026-10-04: "videos.insert (session init) failed (HTTP 400): The request metadata specifies an invalid video description.").**
- **Cause.** Item itm-mut9a0vj-2ktkk1a7, the Gruber / White Rose report, composes to 5,105 bytes. That is the 1,313-byte body, its 37 chapters, and the hashtags and links. YouTube allows 5,000 bytes and no "<" or ">" (Data API, snippet.description). Nothing in ContentStudio knew that, so the calendar's upload sent it and YouTube's vague refusal was all that came back. The other items in that run uploaded.
- **The rule.** It lives in one place, `youtubeDescriptionProblem` in publish-types.ts, mirrored in the renderer's publish.types.ts.
  - It counts UTF-8 bytes, not characters, so an emoji counts 4.
  - The message names the overrun and the ways out: drop or shorten some chapters, turn chapters off for this item, or shorten the body.
  - Nothing is trimmed to fit. No fallback.
- **Where it applies.**
  - `planVideoInsert` (the upload) and `planVideoUpdate` (the push) refuse before anything is sent.
  - The publish panel's Upload and Push buttons give it as their blocked reason.
  - For this item, turning chapters off brings it under the limit.
- **Checks.** routing-publish-checks: the new check covers exactly 5,000, one byte over, emoji at and over the limit, angle brackets and empty. On the real report it says "5,105 bytes … take out at least 105". build:electron and the frontend build are clean.

**259. Fewer chapters: none shorter than a tenth of the video or a minute, merged before titling (Owen, 2026-10-04: "we dont need that many chapters. especially on longer videos").**
- **What was wrong.** On snap at the chapters grain (outline + assign + Viterbi at switch cost 20, then a level-2 sub-outline inside every section past 15 min), long videos got a chapter every one to three minutes. The six latest reports:

  | report | length | chapters | median chapter |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Gruber pt 1 | 63 min | 37 | 78 s |
  | Gruber pt 2 | 75 min | 33 | 108 s |
  | Metaxas intro | 91 min | 27 | 165 s |
  | Kent Hovind | 62 min | 23 | 99 s |
  | Alex Jones | 46 min | 21 | 119 s |
  | f1 Gruber | 15 min | 8 | 113 s |

  Gruber pt 1's chapter list also pushed its description past YouTube's 5,000 bytes (#258).
- **The rule.**
  - `chapters.ts` `consolidateShort`, at `minChapterSeconds`: a tenth of the runtime, never under 60 s.
  - The shortest short chapter merges first, into whichever neighbour is shorter. The merged chapter keeps the longer one's outline label.
  - Sponsor reads (`isAd`) are never merged and never merged into.
  - It runs after refinement and before the titles, so each title is written for its whole merged chapter.
  - A video now carries about ten chapters at most.
- **Owen overrides Law 6 here.** A duration-derived bound on chapter LENGTH now sits beside the declared switch cost (#199, #213). The outline, assign and refinement are unchanged.
- **Not changed.** The stories grain, and the retired whole-transcript engine's prompt bands.
- **Checks.** check:chaptering 55 (one new). build:electron is clean.

It now keeps quotes around a whole option, as curly double quotes, with any trailing "?", "!" or "." after them. A lone quote at one end is still stripped.

  - The inputs picker reads "Chapters (stories past 20 min)" and "Stories (any length)".

**263. Deleting a week is refused while Final Cut Pro is working in it; a partial delete can never be synced over the archive (Owen, 2026-10-06: "i archived a project at the same time i opened final cut pro ... it was changing actively while it was being deleted, because fcpx changes things every time you open it").**
- **What happened.**
  - 11:17:49: "deleting the LOCAL copy of 2026-09-20", archived at /Volumes/iO/FCPX/2026-09-20.
  - Final Cut Pro opened that week's library while it was being deleted and wrote into it, so `fs.promises.rm` failed partway and the handler threw.
  - The throw came before the projects list and the archive ledger were updated, so 2026-09-20 stayed listed and stayed "kept in step". The next launch's drift pass would have rsynced (`--inplace`) the 1.1 MB rebuilt library over the archived one.
- **Fixed by hand.**
  - The week's 3 projects were removed from config/projects.json and its 4 entries from config/archive-synced.json, with copies kept in the session scratchpad.
  - The leftover local folder was deleted after lsof showed Final Cut had nothing open in it.
  - /Volumes/iO/FCPX/2026-09-20 is untouched (2026-09-20.fcpbundle, complete, files, thumbnails).
- **The code (electron/services/editor/final-cut-open.ts, editor-ipc.ts).**
  - **Refused while open.** Both deletes, the local copy and the archive copy, ask lsof which files a Final Cut Pro process has open under the folder, and refuse while it has any, naming the library. An lsof that cannot answer stops the delete by name.
  - **Ledger first.** The local delete now drops the week from the archive ledger BEFORE the first unlink. The archive was just verified identical, and from the first unlink the local folder is a partial copy that must never be synced.
  - **Partial delete.** If the rm fails, or the folder exists again afterwards because something rebuilt it, the week also leaves the projects list. The error says the archive copy is complete and untouched, and names what is left to remove in the Finder after closing it in Final Cut. Retrying the delete could not help: the leftovers fail the re-verification, and syncing them would overwrite the good copy.
- **Checks.** Run live against this Mac: Final Cut had 2026-10-04 open and the refusal named "2026-10-04.fcpbundle (and N of its media files)"; a week it did not have open returned none. check:pure passes and build:electron is clean. No archive keeper exists to extend.

**264. One editor processing run holds one Crucible queue session, from voice isolation to the last track's transcription (coordinating session, 2026-10-06, from the logs: isolation closed its session at 01:19:20, track t0 opened a new one at 01:29:13 and loaded qwen3-asr-1.7b + qwen3-aligner, closed at 01:42:09, and track t1 opened another at 01:42:17 and loaded both again).**
- **Cause.** Each step held the server's session only while it worked: the isolator per track (released on `separation_release`), each transcription job per track. Closing a session settles the card, so every new session reloaded its models (~20–30 s per project), and between steps the card was free for another app.
- **Now.**
  - `electron/crucible/run-session.ts` (new). A `RunSession` is a `SessionSource`: its first step on a server asks the lanes for the session (the ServerSessions rule still holds: an open session of ours is joined, never a second one), every later step gets the same one, and a step's own release lets go of nothing. The run lets go once, when it ends.
  - The isolator takes the run as its session source (`voiceIsolation.open(log, run)`); every transcription job does too (`transcribe(…, run)` → `createAsrResponder` → `serveEditorAsrRequest` → `transcribeOnCrucible({sessions})` → the venue's `session({source})`). The metadata job's path is unchanged (no `source`, the lanes as before).
  - **The hand-off.** Processing and transcription are two IPC calls. The setup modal now sends `transcribeAfter` with the workflow options (main strips it before Python sees them). A run that succeeds with it set is parked, keyed by the zip it produced. The transcription of that zip adopts it. Every other way out lets go: a failed or cancelled workflow, no transcription asked for, a new processing run, a transcription of another zip, the editor window closing, or no transcription within 5 minutes (said in the log by name). A transcription with no parked run (the Transcribe button) holds its own run across its tracks.
  - **The gaps.** After isolation our side runs the rest of the workflow (GCC-PHAT sync, video alignment, Dugan, every compound, the zip), then the renderer's bootstrap, then transcribe.py's extraction and compaction of each track. That was 593 s on 2026-10-06 and grows with the stream, so it can pass `idle_s` 900. While a run holds a session it touches it every 30 s (`SESSION_TOUCH_EVERY_MS`), the rule lanes.ts already uses around cloud calls.
  - **No fallbacks.** A session the server ends under the run is never replaced: the next step fails `session_closed` naming the reason. A step asking after the run let go fails loudly (a wiring bug). Every release sits in the IPC's terminal callbacks (`onComplete`, the pre-spawn catch, the context-build failure).
- **Keeper.** `tools/test-crucible-editor-run.js` (5 checks) drives the app's real wiring (the context's lanes, `crucibleVoiceIsolation`, the ASR venue, `serveEditorAsrRequest`) on the fake. Before: 3 sessions, the transcriber loaded twice. Now: isolation of two tracks + the gap + two tracks = one session asked for, closed once (reason `client`), every job an item of it, `vocals-roformer` and `qwen3-asr-1.7b` each loaded once, touched through the gap and not after. Also: the hand-off deadline and another zip let go of a parked run; a server-ended session fails the next track and nothing reopens. The fake now puts an asr job's model on the card (loaded only when not resident) and records `cardLoads`.
- **Checks.** check:crucible 24 keepers pass, check:asr and check:pure pass, build:electron is clean. Not run: the frontend build (this worktree has no frontend node_modules; the change is one spread into `startWorkflow`'s `any`). No live Crucible, the app not launched.
- **Unsure.** The 5-minute hand-off window is a guess at the renderer's rescan + bootstrap time; a bootstrap slower than that lets go and the transcription opens its own session (loudly logged, still correct). The aligner is modelled riding with the transcriber in the fake; the real server's aligner residency inside one session is assumed, not measured.

**265. The chapter assign stays on the questions form: the items form was measured with no gain (Owen, 2026-10-06: "see if we can make the api calls more efficient").**
- **The measurement.** It was run live on the Mac's qwen3.5-9b (Crucible 1.0.108), the card otherwise idle, with Owen's go-ahead ("lets do the fixes"). The input was the Kid Rock story transcript (2026-10-05, 198 sentences) with its real 19-item outline, asked in 64-sentence batches both ways under one bench session (client `contentstudio-bench`).
  - **Answers:** argmax agreed on 198 of 198 sentences, with mean total-variation distance 0.0000.
  - **Time:** 544 ms per sentence on the questions form against 524 ms on the items form.
- **Why there is no gain.** Since 1.0.78 the server sends questions through the items route itself, so the shared state is already cached.
- **Where the cost is.** About 0.5 s per sentence is the per-item text: 19 option lines plus the sentence and the one before it. The 0.2 s per question seen in serve.log belongs to other question kinds with fewer options and shorter text.
- **Not done.** Making the assign cheaper means changing its prompt, for example the outline once in the state with shorter options. That changes what is measured (YTSeg F1, #199) and is not done without a measurement. The bench script is in the session scratchpad (bench-items.js).

**266. A queue of several videos on one Crucible runs as one stage-major batch: one session, each model loaded once (coordinating session, 2026-10-06, from Owen's 2026-10-03/04 logs: a 6-video queue on the Mac loaded ASR, the 9B and the 27B once PER JOB, 18 loads, the 27B's context growing 8K→32K job to job; built on its branch, checked offline with the fake Crucible, no live server, the app not launched).** The design is the header of `electron/crucible/batch.ts`.
- **What a batch is.** When the queue plan finds two or more startable rows bound for one free server, it plans them as ONE batch (the plan's `start` rows carry `batch: {id, position, of}`); one row runs exactly as before. Each member still runs its own pipeline in its own `generate-metadata` / `send-held-prompt` call, with its own progress, result, report and Stop. Nothing is split or resumed from disk: at each stage boundary the pipeline awaits `enterJobStage(stage, needs)` (lanes.ts), which for a single job only records the stage and for a batch member waits at the turnstile. A job's state between stages stays in its own call frame.
- **The stages, in order:** `transcribe` (asr), `chapters` (snap's boundaries on the 9B, `summarize: false`), `fields` (the lessons, the chapter titles over the stored boundaries, the field calls, the scrub; a compilation's summaries; the whole-transcript engine), `gate-check-N` / `gate-revise-N` (the re-roll gate's rounds), `finish` (thumbnail words and the save). A stage opens when every live member has reached it; one member holds the turn at a time, in plan order. CPU work after transcription (the thumbnails' frame sampling) runs outside any turn (`finishJobStage()` in runPipeline).
- **One session.** The batch shares one queue session (lanes.ts `SessionShare`), asked for by its first GPU work and closed when its last member ends; the cloud-call touch rule is each member's own, on that session.
- **Loaded once, at the largest context.** Each member states at each gate what its stage needs per model (`StageNeed`); when the stage opens, the largest need per model is set as that model's load floor on the session (`CardSession.setLoadFloors`), so the first load of the stage is at the batch's largest context. The scorer's need is exact (the transcript's own chunk states, stage-needs.ts `scorerNeed`); the writing model's is estimated (the real title prompt over the stored boundaries; a field call is its content plus P4's largest measured prompt overhead, 28,000 chars; the scrub 21,000 chars plus 16,384). A call that needs more than its floor grows the load once, said loudly. A row whose model the server cannot bind states no floor, said in the log; its own call still fails by name.
- **Chapter titles move to the fields stage in a batch.** `chaptering/titles.ts` is chapterUnits's step 8 run alone over a boundaries-only result; `check:chaptering` holds the two equal (same chapters, title prompts, warnings, call counts) on a tagged, refined transcript with a run-out title. chaptering.service.ts was not edited (another change was expected there; #265 left it as it was), so its own loop and this one are kept equal by that check.
- **The re-roll gate is round-major** (reroll/gate.ts `runGate`): every field's checks of a round, then every field's rewrites, then their checks, instead of one field's whole loop before the next. A field's rounds read only that field, so what ships is the same; the scorer and the writing models trade the card once per round instead of once per field and round, single jobs included. `phase` is awaited at each check and revise point, which a batch maps to its gate stages. The decide and re-roll records now list calls in round order.
- **Rulings taken here (no Owen ruling yet).** A row added while a batch runs WAITS FOR THE NEXT BATCH (the plan says "starts with the next batch", `waiting[].batchOf`); joining mid-batch would transcribe beside the others' 9B/27B and move floors after a stage opened. A failure or a Stop ends that member only; the rest carry on. A member the renderer never starts is dropped after 30 s. A session the server ends under the batch fails each member's next call by name, as for one job; nothing reopens. A job of several items runs its gate and thumbnail words inside its own fields turn (declared in the log). No cap on batch size.
- **Rows.** A waiting member's row says which stage it waits for and why ("Waiting for the batch's titles, description and tags (a batch of 3 on mac): 1 job is still at chapter finding"), a `waiting` progress line; the stall clock pauses while it waits.
- **Checks.** `tools/test-crucible-batch.js` (new, 5 checks, real lanes/sessions/transport/asr door/snap against the fake): three jobs share one session and the card takes the asr model, the 9B and the 27B once each (the 27B at 32,768, the largest any job stated); every asr job before the first decide and every 9B call before the first 27B call; a failure in one job ends that job only; a Stop while waiting at a gate stops that job only; a row added mid-batch waits for the next, and one row alone runs with no batch; the turnstile's open/turn/floor rules. test-crucible-lanes.js's one-per-server check now plans one row per server (two rows on one server are a batch). check:crucible 25 keepers, check:pure, check:chaptering (56), check:asr, check:reroll (20), check:p4, check:digest, check:retired, check:legacy-gone, check:strays, check:mutes, thumbnail-pipeline-checks (28) pass; build:electron and the frontend build are clean.
- **Not verified.** No keeper drives `MetadataGeneratorService.generate` inside a batch (the keeper drives the pipeline's GPU skeleton through the same modules); the field-stage floor constants are estimates from P4's table; nothing was run against a real server.

**267. Owen's rulings on #266's batches (2026-10-06).**
- **Size: "Cap at 4".** A batch takes at most `MAX_BATCH` = 4 jobs in queue order (lanes.ts plan). The rest wait with "a batch of 4 starts first, and this job starts with the next batch". Keeper: test-crucible-batch.js, 6 checks.
- **A job added while a batch runs: "Wait for next batch".** This is what #266 built.
- **A batch session the server ends: "Fail them, I'll requeue".** Every unfinished member fails naming the reason. Nothing opens a fresh session for them (#255's session rule).

**268. ⌘Q quits for real: after the Crucible quit sweep the app exits itself instead of asking Electron to quit again (Owen, 2026-10-06: "when i hit cmd+q, it closes the window but doesnt close the program. i have to reopen and hit cmd+q again").**
- **What the logs showed.** Every failing quit (2026-10-04 12:02, 2026-10-06 15:45, 16:45, and 16:58 when Owen pressed ⌘Q on a debug launch) logged:
  1. the held pass, "giving back what ContentStudio holds on Crucible first...";
  2. "Quit sweep: 0 hold(s)";
  3. the second pass, "Application is quitting...".
  Then nothing: no `[Quit] will-quit`. The windows closed and the process stayed alive with its lanes `quitting` (#261). The next quit, after `activate` reopened a window, went through.
  A scripted quit (AppleScript `quit`) went through first time, twice, so the trouble is on the menu's ⌘Q path.
- **Most likely cause.** With nothing to sweep, the sweep settles at once. The old `finally → app.quit()` then ran while Electron was still inside the first, prevented, quit, and that one's result marked the app as not quitting. A JS-started quit in a minimal repro did not stall either way, and ⌘Q could not be scripted here (osascript may not send keystrokes), so this is the reading, not a reproduction.
- **The fix.** It does not depend on that reading. Once the sweep settles, main.ts runs the local cleanup itself (`releaseOnQuit`: the archive rsync stopped, the Crucible loops stopped) and calls `app.exit(0)`. Electron's quit state is no longer involved.
  - The other `before-quit` handlers (thumbnails' text-model release) already ran on the held pass.
  - `will-quit`'s hotkey release is skipped, because the OS releases a global shortcut when its process exits.
  - The log now ends "[Quit] exiting (exit code 0)".
- **Checks.** A dev launch quit by AppleScript logged the sweep, "Application is quitting...", "[Quit] exiting (exit code 0)", and the process was gone. check:crucible passes (25 keepers) and build:electron is clean. Owen's ⌘Q on the next build is the real test.

**270. One video's independent model calls go to Crucible together, and a batch's cloud work no longer waits for the turn (Owen, 2026-10-06: "Yes, where independent"; coordinating session, from Owen's batch of 4 at 17:33–17:36, whose fields all route to `claude -p` and were written one video at a time while the Mac sat idle).**
- **The fact.** Every 9B/27B engine Crucible runs serves 16 chats side by side (vLLM `--max-num-seqs 16`, mlx-lm `--decode-concurrency 16`; crucible-pc-1). Until now a video's field calls went one at a time: the lane's SLOT let one GPU call run per server, and the cloud calls were awaited in a loop.
- **The dependency map** (written where the scheduling lives, metadata-tasks.ts `runUnitsTogether`):
  - before the fields: the channel lessons (the titles call carries their insights block); the chapters and their titles (out of scope: each title call reads the ones before it).
  - titles, pinned comment, spoken keywords, chapterless tags, the description: each reads only the transcript or digest, the chapters, the pools. Independent.
  - thumbnail text reads the KEPT titles (after the titles' grounding re-ask). It waits for them.
  - after the fields: code-assembled tags and hashtags; the scrub (reads the finished description, hook, alternates and chapter titles; its plans read only their own field); the re-roll gate (reads the scrubbed text; a field's rounds read only that field).
- **What now goes together.**
  - The field units: every unit starts once what it reads exists. One local model's units at a time, in plan order (so each model loads once, as before); cloud units at once. Before a local model's group goes, the job's sessions are told how many calls of what largest size are coming (`JobSessions.expect`): the first call loads at the group's largest size.
  - The scrub's plans.
  - The re-roll gate: a round's checks of every field together (and a field's decide batches), then every field's rewrites together (and a field's per-rule calls). Rounds stay rounds and per field.
  - Cap: `JOB_CALLS_PER_SERVER` = 16 (crucible/fan-out.ts), the engine width.
- **Outputs are the loop's.** fan-out.ts `settleTogether` waits for every call it sent, then the caller reads the answers in plan order: merged fields, warnings, `_prompt_trace` (the door's trace push and `claude -p`'s go through `inOrder`), the gate's records, the scrub's failures. The first failure in plan order is thrown as itself; a unit whose input failed, or that had not started when a failure came, is not sent. Known difference: when two fields fail in one run, the one named may differ from the sequential loop's.
- **The lane's SLOT** (lanes.ts): up to 16 calls of ONE admitted job on ONE model hold it together (key: job + model). The same job's call on another model waits for them; a standalone call, or another job's, takes it alone as before, and once one waits, no further call of the running group joins past it (it now waits for the group's calls in flight, at most 16, instead of one).
- **The session** (session.ts `CardSession.admit`): every local call is counted while it is answered.
  - No load under an answer: a call that needs a load (another model, or this one grown) waits, holding the session's lock, until every call in flight has finished.
  - The width question: before a call is admitted beside k others, the server is asked `GET /v1/capability?class=generate&context_tokens=<largest size in flight>&concurrency=<k+1>` (Crucible's own accounting, weights + overhead + KV per token × context × concurrency, docs/FITS-AND-THE-CARD.md; loads are still asked at one in flight). Where the ceiling row is under the size, the call waits for one in flight to finish, said once in the log. Answers are remembered per session and model. On the Mac (64 GiB, 3 GiB desktop allowance) the 27B-8bit's terms give about 62,600 tokens per request at 6 in flight, so the fields' 24,576 fits.
- **Batches: the call turn** (batch.ts, the coordinator's rule). `transcribe` and `chapters` keep the stage turn. Every later stage opens for every member at once once all have reached it; a member takes the turn only for its LOCAL calls (`StageBatch.takeCall`, from `aiCall`), shared by its calls in flight together and given back when its last local call ends. A cloud-only member never takes it; a mixed member's cloud calls never hold it.
  - Why the boundary is the call, not the needs stated at the gate: a stated need is a load-floor estimate (a compilation states none and still loads the 27B); a GPU call through the lane is exactly the work that needs the card.
  - To avoid trading models back and forth: a member whose next local call follows its last within the same event-loop turn keeps the turn; when the turn is free, a waiter for the model the batch used last goes first, then the lowest stage, then plan order.
  - The stall clock's pause is now counted, so a member waiting for the turn with its own cloud calls running does not arm it.
- **Expected saving** (from Owen's logs, not measured after the change). Kid Rock, 2026-10-06 16:04, every field on `claude -p` Opus: three field calls of 24.7, 10.7 and 11.8 s (47 s) become about 25 s; three scrub calls of 3.6, 5.7 and 5.1 s become about 6 s; about 30 s an item. Owen's batch of 4: its fields stage ran serially across videos and now runs side by side, about 3 min saved on the batch. Local 27B routing: decode overlaps on the engine; how much prefill overlaps on mlx-lm is not measured.
- **Checks.** New keeper tools/test-crucible-fan-out.js (6), real lanes, sessions, transport, AI manager and units on the fake:
  - titles, description and cloud tags are in flight together; the thumbnail text waits for the kept titles; the 9B waits for the 27B; merged fields, warnings and trace are in plan order though answered out of order; each model loads once, never under an answer.
  - Stop hangs up on all three in-flight calls.
  - a failed titles call fails the item with its own error after its siblings settle; nothing that reads it, and no later model, is sent.
  - the slot's first-come rules; growth waits for the call in flight, and an expectation loads once; the width question narrows 3 to 2.
  - test-crucible-batch.js (8): a cloud-only fields stage across 3 videos has all 6 cloud calls in flight together with no member holding the turn; a mixed stage runs one video's local call at a time beside the others' cloud calls.
  - reroll-checks (21): a round's checks and rewrites overlap, and an out-of-order run yields the identical item, record and trace. scrub-reroll-checks: the plans overlap, and trace, failures and item keep plan order.
  - The fake Crucible counts answers in flight (`answering`, `answerLog`, `peakAnswering`, `loadsUnderAnswers`), can hold them (`holdAnswer`), and states ceilings per concurrency.
  - check:crucible 26 keepers, check:pure, check:reroll (21), check:chaptering (56), check:asr, check:p4, check:digest, check:retired, check:legacy-gone, check:strays, check:mutes and thumbnail-pipeline-checks (28) pass; build:electron is clean. The frontend was not touched. No live Crucible, the app not launched.
- **Open.** Narrowing on a refused width (waiting) rather than failing is a declared choice awaiting Owen's ruling. A sibling of a failed field is waited for, not aborted. The description writes one candidate (`DESCRIPTION_CANDIDATES` = 1), so it gains nothing inside its own unit. The gate's decide calls state no group floor (a larger state grows the load after the calls in flight finish). The local Crucible manifest gives the Mac 27B-8bit `--decode-concurrency 8`, not 16; past the engine's width the server queues.

**273. OBS sets go through the vMix pipeline unchanged, except that their captures get no device drift (Owen, 2026-10-07: "we're keeping the vmix mode as is. is obs mode necessary since things are so similar?").**
- **No OBS mode.** Utility Suite (Projects\VideoOrganizer on owens-pc) turns OBS's output into a set named exactly like a vMix one: `<set> master`, `cam`, `cam 2`, `screen capture`, `game capture`, and `mic audio` / `mic 2 audio` / `screen audio` / `game audio` WAVs taken losslessly from the program recording's PCM tracks. The existing detection, sync and generators read it as they are.
  - Owen: the filenames stay vMix-compatible; whatever differs goes in the sync file.
- **The one difference.** The `vmix_sources` speed factor (0.9999763884) is applied without measuring the file. It was measured on vMix's capture clocks. OBS records every source on one clock, frame-exact against the master (Owen's Source Record fork; the drift is fixed in its C code). On an OBS set the factor would add about 0.25 s over three hours.
- **How a set is told apart.** By `<set> sync.json` beside the master, the sync report the fork writes. When it is there, screen, game and cam 2 are held at an explicit driftFactor 1.0 (identity), through the same channel as a manual alignment.
  - A driftFactor set by hand still wins.
  - Offsets are still measured as before.
  - A file by that name that is not a sync report (no `frame_interval_ns` / `sources`) stops the run.
- **Fixed on the way.** The GS Solo and SSB Solo generators never received `video_drift_factors`. They always took the vMix stretch, so even a manual driftFactor never reached those two compounds. Both now take it.
  - They also took no `video_offsets`; fixed in #274.
- **Checks.** py_compile is clean. `_is_obs_set` was exercised on no report, a valid report and a malformed one. `calculate_retime_map(speed_factor=1.0)` gives an identity timeMap. No live set has been processed; Owen tests.

**274. Solo compounds take the measured video offsets, and a video file in an audio slot gives its audio (Owen, 2026-10-07: "ok. continue", the two items #273 left open).**
- **Solo GS and SSB placed every dedicated source at 0s.** The measured or manual cam1, screen and game offsets reached only the dual-camera compounds, so the solo ones showed each source as many frames off as the measurement had corrected in the dual ones.
  - Both now place them through `FCPXMLUtils.solo_video_delay`, using the same 29.97 frame rounding as the dual compounds.
  - A positive tau delays the clip.
  - A negative tau cannot move a clip left of a compound that starts at 0s (the dual compounds have a 60-frame lead-in for that). Instead it trims the clip's in-point (`start`) by |tau|, which shows the same source frame at the same instant.
  - A crop of the master is never shifted.
- **An audio slot holding a video was synced as a video.** The setup modal offers video files for audio slots ("the pipeline extracts the audio track from one"). But `sync_file` chose by extension, so a camera file in a mic slot was re-encoded whole with libx264 into `<stem>_processed.mp4`, and that file became the "audio" source.
  - The workflow's audio loop now passes `audio_only=True`, which always yields `<stem>_processed.wav`. That is the name the modal and the reuse check already expect.
  - A video with no audio stream given as audio is an error.
  - `apply_sync_to_audio` passes `-vn`.
- **Not changed.** The modal still lets one file feed only one slot, so one camera file cannot be both a video source and a mic source. Utility Suite hands OBS sets lossless WAVs, so nothing needs that.
- **Checks.**
  - py_compile is clean.
  - `solo_video_delay` gives ("0s", None) for 0 and sub-half-frame tau, a frame-rounded offset for positive tau, and a frame-rounded `start` for negative tau.
  - `sync_file(audio_only=True)` on a generated H.264+AAC clip gave a pcm_s24le `cam_processed.wav` with the 0.1 s override applied.
  - The same call on a clip with no audio stream raised.
  - No live session was processed; Owen tests.

**275. An OBS set is told by Utility Suite's manifest, and its Mic 2 and game audio come from inside the recordings (obs-setup-pc's final OBS-set design, approved by Owen 2026-10-07).**
- **The set Utility Suite delivers.** The filenames are exactly the vMix-era ones:
  - `<set> master`, `cam` (Lumix + Mic 1), `cam 2` (90D + Mic 2), `screen capture` (+ Mac) and `game capture` (+ game audio), all Hybrid MP4 straight from OBS.
  - Three lossless WAVs from the main recording: `mic audio`, `screen audio` and `master audio`.
  - There is no `mic 2 audio` or `game audio` WAV.
  - The one OBS addition is `<set> sync.json`. It is now a manifest: `"format": "obs-set"`, version 1, and `files` gives each present file's `role` (cam, cam2, screen, game, master, mic1-audio, …), its final filename and its `offset_frames` against master.mp4.
- **#273's check followed the old report and would have stopped every OBS set.** It required `frame_interval_ns` and `sources`, and the manifest has `files`. `_is_obs_set` now requires `format` "obs-set" and version 1, and anything else at that name is an error.
- **Mic 2 and game audio exist only inside `cam 2.mp4` and `game capture.mp4`.**
  - `auto-detect-audio` reads the manifest (`obsSetEmbeddedAudio` in session-sources.ts). It fills mic1 from cam, mic2 from cam2, screen from screen and game from game, but only where no WAV was detected. The WAVs are lossless and the recordings' audio is AAC, so in practice it adds mic2 and game.
  - A manifest that is unreadable, of another format or version, or that lists a file not in the folder is an error. It never counts as "no manifest", because that would silently drop Mic 2.
  - The processing side already syncs a video in an audio slot as audio (#274).
- **One file, one source of each kind.** The setup modal refused a file in two slots. It now refuses only two audio slots or two video slots on one file, so `cam 2.mp4` can be cam 2's picture and Mic 2's sound. Nothing in processing collides: screen, game and cam videos are never rendered, and the audio becomes `<stem>_processed.wav`.
- **Not used.** The manifest's offsets: audio is still measured by GCC-PHAT and video by picture, as on a vMix set. Video roles are still found by filename, because the names are vMix's.
- **Checks.**
  - build:electron is clean and the frontend tsc (tsconfig.app.json) is clean.
  - A fake set folder with the manifest: the embedded audio map gave mic1/mic2/screen/game, and the video patterns found cam, cam 2, screen capture and game capture.
  - A listed file missing raised, a wrong format raised, and no manifest returned null.
  - py_compile is clean. thumbnail-pipeline-checks (28) and check:pure pass.
  - No live set has been processed; Owen tests.

**276. A camera quadrant showing a frozen picture is an inactive camera, so OBS's "No Signal" card no longer reads as a live Cam 2 (Owen, 2026-10-08: "does that mean its going to try to keep cam 2 active? or will it detect no pixel changes and leave it disabled").**
- **The miss.** The hybrid compounds decide solo vs dual camera by sampling the master's top-right quadrant (`CameraDetector._is_camera_active`).
  - The detector called a quadrant inactive only when it was a solid colour: vMix's black (never on) or blue (turned off).
  - OBS shows a colour-bar "No Signal" card for a camera that is off (overall std 215).
  - On the 2026-10-08 master, where the 90D was off for all 3 h 25 m, the detector called Cam 2 ACTIVE at every point sampled. The hybrids would have shown the test card throughout.
  - Clearing the Cam 2 slot does not help, because the detector reads the master, not the slot.
- **The rule.** After the solid-colour tests, a quadrant whose picture changes by less than 0.25 is inactive. Change is the mean |difference| in grey level at 240×135 between frames 2 s apart (`STATIC_DIFF_THRESHOLD`, `STATIC_GAP_SECONDS`).
  - Measured on the "No Signal" card: 0.001–0.03.
  - Measured on live cameras: 2.29–37 (Cam 1 on the 2026-10-08 OBS master and on three vMix masters, 2026-08-05, 09-30 and 10-07).
  - One vMix reading of 0.00 was a solid-black frame (camera off), which the solid-black test catches first.
- **Checks.**
  - On the 2026-10-08 master, top-right is inactive at 600 s, 7200 s and 12305 s. The live game quadrant (bottom-right) is active at the same points.
  - The recent vMix masters' top-right is still inactive (solid black, unchanged path).
  - No dual-camera vMix stream is on disk, so a live Cam 2 was checked by proxy through the live Cam 1 quadrant.
